# open_python-audit — the Python estate: what is wrong, and the backlog that fixes it

## Where this stands after batch 9 (2026-08-30)

**The backlog is drained down to its residue: of 304 findings, 2 are OPEN, 43 PARTIAL, 224 FIXED,
11 OWNER, 20 DISSOLVED, 2 OBSOLETE, 2 WRONG.** Batch 9 (`cc13982f`) took the last 56 OPEN rows; every
one of them was re-read against the tree before a status was written here, which is how two of them
came back OPEN and one came back OWNER.

**No high-severity finding is OPEN.** All six remaining highs are PARTIAL with their surviving site
named in the row — `DUP-03`, `DUP-04`, `ING-01`, `ingest-flow-04`, `MED-002`, `X4`. The two rows still
OPEN are both **medium** and both are named work rather than unknown work:

- **`CAT-CORE-13`** — the catalog's single `Settings` class, now 432 lines, still carrying every
  domain's configuration. Reported drained in batch 9 and untouched in the tree.
- **`DUP-15`** — the Dapr-workflow scheduler written twice, whose two copies have drifted further
  apart (3.0 s vs 5.0 s, and only one of them can tell a slow schedule from a failed one).

**The remaining 43 rows are PARTIAL survivors and 11 OWNER decisions, not undrained work.** Every
PARTIAL row names, from the code, exactly what did not close — a second site, a residue, a gate that
measures the wrong thing — and every OWNER row carries a question written out for the owner rather
than a paraphrase. Six of the PARTIALs were created by batch 9 itself, on purpose, in preference to
rounding a partial fix up (`CAT-CORE-09`, `ANN-07`, `ANN-14`, `MAINT-12`, `SKG-11`, `DUP-21`); one of
those, `SKG-11`, records a defect the FIX introduced. See **Ledger corrections (2026-08-30, batch 9)**.

**This file is not yet deletable**, and the bar for deleting it is the header's own: the backlog
drained, not the OPEN column empty. Forty-five rows still describe something true about the code.

## How this was produced (so you can trust or re-run it)

Every skill reference was read in full **before** any code was opened — `fastapi` (13 refs),
`writing-python` (10), `python-infrastructure` (5), `openfga`, `testing-python`, plus the vendored
`rask-architecture`, `rask-services-fleet` and `rask-lance-catalog` skills. Those references, plus the
estate's own standing rulings (Dapr over raw components; no app relational DB; secrets from the Dapr
store fail-closed; `packages/storage` as the only boto3 home; the two sanctioned service layouts), were
compiled into a 17-rule calibration sheet that every auditor was held to — so that, for example, a plain
`def` route doing blocking work is **not** reported (FastAPI threadpools it), while the same work inside
`async def` is.

14 auditors then read **every `.py` file** in `services/` (12 services) and `packages/` (7 packages) —
55 769 + 22 563 lines — one auditor per coherent scope, plus a cross-service structure mapper and a
dedicated duplication hunter. Every finding was then handed to a **separate adversarial verifier** whose
brief was to refute it: open the cited lines, judge the evidence, and reject anything sanctioned or
mischaracterised. 30 agents, ~6.5 M tokens, 1 713 tool calls.

**Evidence convention.** Every finding below carries `file:line` sites that were opened twice — once by
the auditor, once by the verifier. `CONFIRMED` = both passes reproduced the claim. `ADJUSTED` = the
mechanism reproduced but the severity or framing was corrected by the verifier (the corrected form is
what appears here). Refuted findings were dropped and are not listed. Where a verifier spotted something
the auditor missed, it is filed under **Unfiled extras** at the end of each group table.

## Ledger corrections (2026-08-30, batch 9)

**Batch 9's code shipped in `cc13982f`: 56 findings attempted, and the commit's own accounting was
49 approved / 7 rejected.** Re-read against the tree, it is **47 closed / 1 to the owner / 6 PARTIAL /
2 still OPEN**. The seven rejections the commit declared are all real and are recorded at their true
status; the corrections below are the three things a code-read found that the drain's own list did not
say, plus the two rows it counted as drained without touching.

**One fix left the estate RED and had to be partly reverted.** `ANN-07` changed `fire_task_event` to
`-> Task` returning `Task.model_validate(updated)`. The actor returns a PARTIAL document — the
transition it just wrote, without `source`/`media`, which live on a task record the actor never reloads
— so FastAPI validated the response against a model the payload cannot satisfy and three integration
tests failed `ResponseValidationError`. The revert is in `cc13982f` itself, and the finding's own gate
(`services/annotator/tests/test_routes_declare_their_response_shape.py:209-215`) carries a DOCUMENTED
exemption for `post /tasks/{task_id}/events` saying why. Thirteen other routes did get named schemas
out of the new `annotator/api/v1/responses.py`; the row names both halves. **PARTIAL, not closed on an
unsound model.**

**One fix INTRODUCED a defect HEAD did not have.** `SKG-11`'s bound and eviction are real and
RED-proven, but `_TtlCache` is unsynchronised and `warehouse_registry.project_root` is called from
threadpool workers (`medallion/services/transform.py:567`, `produce.py:114`). `get` does a
read-then-`del self._entries[key]` while `set` REBINDS `self._entries` to a filtered copy, so a
concurrent expiry raises `KeyError` out of a cache READ; the eviction loop's `min(self._entries, ...)`
can raise `RuntimeError: dictionary changed size during iteration`; and two concurrent `set`s lose one
another's entry. The bare module-level `dict` it replaced had none of these — `dict.get` and
`dict[key] = v` are each one atomic bytecode under the GIL. The reported PARTIAL did not mention it;
the row now does. **A fix that trades an unbounded cache for a racy one is not a smaller version of the
fix, it is a different defect**, and it is filed as such rather than as a residue.

**One finding was reported drained while untouched.** `DUP-15` — both schedulers survive and have
drifted FURTHER: `ingest/__init__.py:248 _DaprWorkflowStarter` at a 3.0 s bound treating a timeout as
final, `flows/lifespan.py:163 DaprFlowScheduler` at 5.0 s with a three-outcome settle protocol
(`ScheduleUnconfirmed`, an engine state probe, a late-arrival callback) that ingest has no equivalent
of — so the copy that can double-run a graph is the one that was NOT improved. The commit says so
itself; it is recorded here because the scorecard had already counted it.

**Two further rows the drain's list did not name, found by this pass rather than reported by it.**
Both are the same failure the campaign has caught eight times before — a row rounded up to closed:

- **`CAT-CORE-13` is still OPEN, and nothing in the tree names it.** It was inside batch 9's
  "catalog (19)". `services/catalog/src/catalog/core/config.py` still declares exactly one class
  (`grep -n '^class '` → `23:class Settings(BaseSettings):`) and the file has GROWN again — 432 lines,
  against 391 on 2026-08-24 and the 340 in the original finding. `grep -rn 'CAT-CORE-13' --include=*.py`
  returns nothing: no code, no comment, no test. Recorded OPEN.
- **`ANN-13` is an OWNER decision, not a closure.** The four prefix shapes are unchanged at HEAD
  (`/api`, `/api/jobs`, bare `/projects`, bare `/tasks`). What shipped pins the published surface
  rather than unifying it, and the gate says so in its own first paragraph: "This file does not resolve
  the split; it makes it visible and gated." The split is defensible — `/api/*` is the gateway-published
  media plane, `/projects`/`/tasks` the actor plane the annotator zone's SSR calls directly — so
  unifying is a wire change for two `.remote.ts` files plus a gateway row. That is an owner's call, and
  the row now carries the question written out rather than a FIXED that would hide it.

**Two closures are in the WORKING TREE, not in `cc13982f`, and the rows say so.** `PS-03`
(`runners/htr`'s `fetch_image` retry, now a jittered, time-bounded `tenacity` policy with
`reraise=True`) and `X3` (`known-first-party`) are both fixed on disk and both absent from HEAD —
`git show HEAD:runners/htr/src/htr/iiif.py` still carries the `time.sleep` loop and the control-flow
`assert`, and `git show HEAD:pyproject.toml` still carries the nine-name list. They are recorded FIXED
under this file's standing convention of auditing the tree as it sits, with the discrepancy named in
the closure line so the next reader is not surprised by a git log that does not mention them.

**What did NOT need correcting.** The other 47 closures were re-read against the code before their
status was written — every one has a mechanism visible in the tree and a named pinning test, and the
five that could only have been verified by counting (`catalog-api-11`'s fan-outs, `catalog-api-12`'s
batch checks, `ANN-08`'s bounded pool, `MAINT-04`'s two lanes, `DUP-21`'s medallion half) were checked
at the call site rather than from the test name.

## Ledger corrections (2026-08-30, batch 8)

**Batch 8's code shipped in `14db0444`; its ledger did not, for a day.** 67 findings were drained and
committed while this file still read 118 OPEN — the same class of defect the 2026-08-29 section below
was commissioned to fix, repeated one commit later. Closed here: every batch-8 row was re-read against
HEAD before a status was written (the drain's own list was NOT taken on trust), the scorecard was
recounted by counting rows, and the severity line recomputed. Three things the re-read found, none of
them softenable:

- **FOUR FINDINGS WERE DEFERRED ON A BLOCKER THAT DID NOT EXIST.** `SKG-08`, `SKG-11`, `SKG-13` and
  `SKG-17` were reported out-of-scope because sibling agents were said to hold
  `service_kit/lakehouse/protection.py`, `warehouse_registry.py`, `sources.py` and `sinks.py`.
  `git diff` showed all four files untouched by anyone, so there was no contention to defer around.
  The deferrals were DISCLOSED rather than hidden, which is why they are downgrades and not failures —
  but they are recorded **OPEN**, not counted as drained, and each row now says the stated reason did
  not hold. All four files are still unmodified at HEAD.
- **ONE FINDING WAS CLAIMED FIXED ON A SUBSET.** `SKG-10` was reported FIXED for removing bare env
  reads while `governed/` and `lakehouse/` still carried them. It is recorded **PARTIAL** with the
  survivors named — this campaign has already had to re-open seven findings that were rounded up from
  a subset, and that is the cheapest possible place to catch the eighth. (The verifier's own survivor
  list named `dapr_auth.py` 48/105/117/281; those four are gone at HEAD, because the fix was reworked
  after that verdict. The row names what the CODE shows, not what the verdict said.)
- **Five more were recorded PARTIAL with the surviving site named** rather than rounded up:
  `PS-02` (the taxonomy is applied inside `packages/storage`; `runners/htr`'s inline `ClientError`
  mapping survives), `SK-03` (the pooled catalog client exists; `annotator/projects/lakehouse.py:213`
  still builds its own), `ING-14` (the repeat-poll is memoized; the unfiltered `/runs` fetch survives on
  the first ask), `VS-18` (the four envelope routes are typed; the bare-hit routes cannot be without a
  wire-shape change the zones read), `F-LIN-07` (the three domain values are typed; the `age.run_cypher`
  driver seam and the repository's positional tuples survive). Two further rows moved to **OWNER** with
  the question written out rather than paraphrased: `PS-07` (may `packages/storage` take a
  pydantic-settings dependency, given two sealed runners hold it as a path dep with their own locks?)
  and `PS-09`, which was already OWNER and is now sharper — the drain invested four files' worth of work
  in `packages/tracker` without anyone answering whether it should exist. Consumers at HEAD: still zero.

One correction runs the other way, and it belongs here too: `VS-09`'s 2026-08-28 cell said the fix
"needs a design decision static reading cannot settle" and named an owner decision or a live repro as
the unblock. Batch 8 answered both halves in code — the origin became configuration, the path moved to
the route this service actually serves, and the caller's own bearer now rides the loopback fetch — so
the row is FIXED rather than waived, and the evidence cell records how each blocker was settled.

## Ledger corrections (2026-08-29)

**A commit message claimed 26 closures the file did not carry (2026-08-29).** `d3dd4452`'s message
lists 26 findings as FIXED. At the moment it was written the file carried **14** — the batch-4 rows.
The 12 batch-5 rows (`ingest-flow-15/17/18`, `F-LIN-13/14/15/16`, `ANN-17/18/19/20`,
`COMPUTE-UNBOUNDED-QUERY-PARAMS`) had their CODE committed in the same push and were genuinely fixed
in the tree, but their rows were never flipped: the ledger pass ran before batch 5 finished, and the
two were conflated when the message was written. The code was right and the record was wrong — the
same class of defect the re-audit above was commissioned to find, committed while correcting it.
Closed here: the 12 rows are flipped against the tree (each verified present before being written),
the scorecard is recounted (130→118 OPEN, 107→119 FIXED), and the severity line is recomputed
(164→152, matching OPEN+PARTIAL). `F-LIN-14` is included: its row lagged a further pass because the
agent flipping it hit a session limit mid-file.


An adversarial re-audit of the drain campaign found the LEDGER — not the code — was its least
trustworthy artefact, and this section is the record of putting that right. Nothing here re-opens a
fix; it re-opens claims.

- **One convention for the dated evidence tables, applied everywhere.** The per-scope
  "2026-08-24 evidence" tables in Appendix A are a **historical snapshot** measured at `871b5e14`.
  During the drain, batch commits (`4426d931`, `76f01dcc`, `55bc6a20`, `0c1bb730`, `90767372`)
  rewrote 59 status cells INSIDE that snapshot to FIXED/OWNER/WRONG while the cells' own prose still
  asserted the defect, and single-row commits left their cells alone — two conventions in one
  sitting, both wrong somewhere. All 59 cells are restored to their 2026-08-24 values, and every
  closure (those 59 plus the 11 rows the single-row commits flipped only in the main tables) is now
  an **appended dated closure line** in the evidence cell, naming the closing mechanism and commit.
  The live status lives in the epic and Appendix A tables; the snapshot says what was true on
  2026-08-24 and, dated, what happened since.
- **Three mislabelled refutations relabelled** (owner rulings, 2026-08-29): `CAT-CORE-08` WRONG →
  **PARTIAL (low)** — its mechanism reproduces at HEAD and the WRONG label came from reverting a fix
  attempt that broke 5 gc tests, not from refuting the finding; `ANN-05` WRONG → **OWNER** — the
  refutation cited a code comment, and a comment is not an owner ruling (the fail-open-vs-fail-closed
  question is still unanswered); `ING-09` WRONG → **DISSOLVED** — the duplicated code moved into a
  sealed runner, which is dissolution, not an auditor error.
- **One false FIXED demoted:** `ING-18` → **PARTIAL** — two of its three sites survive
  (the dead hand-clamp at ingest/api.py:470 under the :444 comment claiming it was replaced, and the
  frozen-model idiom at :550). The other findings the re-audit flagged as closed on a subset of their
  sites — `MAINT-15`, `CAT-CORE-10`, `ANN-16`, `PS-06`, `F-LIN-12` — were finished by the 2026-08-29
  working-tree wave and re-verified in the tree before their FIXED stood; each snapshot row records
  what completed them.
- **`cb1c6b11` was a half-commit.** It imported `service_kit.lancekit.arrow_ipc` before that module
  existed — the module arrived four commits later in `ccd62619` — so at `cb1c6b11`'s own tree the
  catalog was uncollectable and the commit's "catalog suite green" claim was false. The code ended up
  correct once `ccd62619` landed; the ledger records the claim was not.
- **The Scorecard above was rebuilt by counting rows**, not by trusting the drain commits' arithmetic.
  Its previous claims (250/30/22/2, "692 commits closed 22 of 304" as the current state) had survived
  40 commits of drain unchanged.

## What the audit did *not* find (read this before the backlog)

The estate is in better shape than the volume above suggests, and several house rules hold **perfectly**:

- **Zero** legacy `Optional[…]` / `Union[…]` across 78 000 lines — the whole estate is on `X | None`
  and builtin generics. *(Re-verified 2026-08-24: still true. But this claim covered only half the rule
  — `type-safety.md` also makes PEP 695 the form for type PARAMETERS, and 19 legacy `TypeVar(` /
  `ParamSpec(` sites survive. Filed as `WP-01` in Appendix E.)*
- **Zero** default-arg `Depends()` — every dependency is an `Annotated[T, Depends(...)]` alias.
- **Zero** `import requests`. **Three** `print()` in the whole estate *(was one on 2026-08-07 — it grew by two)*. `boto3` appears in service code
  in exactly two catalog modules, both already filed (`CAT-CORE-11`, `DUP-04`); everything else goes
  through `packages/storage`.
- **Three** `@dataclass` value objects (all filed: `ANN-15`, `CAT-CORE-16`, `PS-14`) and **no genuine**
  Pydantic-v1 idiom — the `.dict()` calls in `ray-kit` are forced by Ray shipping a V1 `JobDetails`,
  and the code says so at the call site.
- **No** `@app.on_event`; every service uses an `@asynccontextmanager` lifespan.
- **No** relational database in a fleet service (the P7a ruling holds; lineage's AGE store is the
  sanctioned exception), **no** Redis, **no** `BackgroundTasks` used for pageable work. *(Re-verified:
  still true, and lineage's psycopg pool is textbook-correct — `age.py:48` sets
  `check=AsyncConnectionPool.check_connection` (psycopg's `pool_pre_ping`) and `open=False` so it is
  built in the lifespan, not at import.)*
- The catalog's 163 `run_in_threadpool` calls show the blocking-IO rule is understood — E2 is a set of
  sites that were missed when a route gained an `await`, not a service-wide misunderstanding.

The problems are concentrated in four places: **authorization coverage** (E1), **the durable-work planes**
(E3), **configuration sprawl** (E5), and **the absence of a factory for the lance-service layout**, which
is what makes eight entrypoints drift from each other (E6).

## Execution order

| Wave | Epics | Why this order |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **1 — stop the bleeding** | E1, E2, E9 | Open doors and event-loop stalls are exploitable/observable today; E9 is 2 lines of `testpaths` that make the other waves' regressions visible at all. |
| **2 — make failures honest** | E3, E4 | Durable-work correctness and the error contract. Do E4's fail-open items with E3 — they are the same incidents from two directions. |
| **3 — collapse the sprawl** | E5, E6 | Settings-per-service first (it is the precondition for `make_lance_service_app`), then the shared-platform extraction. |
| **4 — shape and speed** | E7, E8, E10, E11 | God-module splits are safest once the contracts above are pinned by tests. |
| **5 — sweep** | E12 | Deletions last, so nothing is deleted that a wave-3 refactor turns out to need. |

## The epics

### E1 — Open doors and mishandled credentials

**P0** · 26 issues (8 high, 12 medium, 6 low)

Authorization that is missing, asymmetric, or forgeable, plus secrets that leave the secret store. Every item here is reachable from the network by someone who should not reach it.

#### The high-severity items in this epic

<details><summary><b>FLOWS-REDOS-ON-LOOP</b> [**FIXED 2026-08-25**] — Caller-supplied regex is compiled and executed on the event loop — a `regex` node freezes the whole flows process <i>(flows-fleet, security, effort M)</i></summary>

**Sites:** `services/flows/src/flows/executor.py:247`, `services/flows/src/flows/executor.py:253`, `services/flows/src/flows/executor.py:251`, `services/flows/src/flows/executor.py:167`, `services/flows/src/flows/executor.py:327` *(+1 more)*

**Why it matters.** `/api/*` has no auth in front of it (documented: "No auth, no app middleware"), so one `POST /api/flows/runs` with a 40-character seed takes the flows pod out until the liveness probe kills it — and `/api/health` is `async def` on the same frozen loop, so the probe fails too. `alto_lines`'s `.*?`-DOTALL scan (line 117) and `compare_texts` are the same class of unbounded CPU work in the same async path, just without an attacker-chosen exponent.

**Fix.** Run every pure-CPU node arm off the loop — `await run_in_threadpool(...)` (or `asyncio.to_thread`) inside the `regex`/`alto`/`compare`/`extract` arms of `dispatch` — and bound the regex itself: cap the subject length, and either reject nested-quantifier patterns or move the match behind an `asyncio.wait_for` on a thread. A thread does not stop the GIL stall, so the length cap is the load-bearing half. `re` alternatives with linear-time guarantees (`google-re2`) are the durable fix if user regex stays a product feature.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** services/flows/src/flows/executor.py: _regex is a plain sync def (:238) reached from `case "regex": return _regex(node, inputs)` inside `async def dispatch` (:168 — auditor said :167, one off), compiled at :246 (auditor said :247), finditer at :253, sub at :251. Call chain verified: routes.create_run awaits executor.execute (:116) -> asyncio.gather(run_node) (:327) -> await dispatch -> sync…


**Re-verified 2026-08-24 — PARTIAL (severity now none).** Every pure-CPU arm now runs off the loop, exactly as the epic's Fix prescribed. executor.py:171 `return Payload(text="\n".join(await asyncio.to_thread(alto_lines, text)))`; :173 `return await asyncio.to_thread(_extract, node, inputs)`; :175 `return await asyncio.to_thread(_regex, node, inputs)`; :182 `return Payload(text=await asyncio.to_thread(compare_texts, texts))`. The load-bearing half — the subject cap — is present: executor.py:250 `_REGEX_MAX_SUBJECT = 256 * 1024` and :260-261 `if len(payload.text) > _REGEX_MAX_SUBJECT: raise NodeError(f"regex input too large: ...")`. Pinned by services/flows/tests/test_executor.py:472-477 (`big = Payload(text="a" * (_REGEX_MAX_SUBJECT + 1))` / `pytest.raises(NodeError, match="too large")`). Fixed by df52e874 `fix(async): the five confirmed blocking-on-the-event-loop defects (E2 batch)`, which cites this finding id in the code comment at executor.py:163-168. The comment is honest that a thread does not stop a GIL stall and names the cap as the real defence. [refuted-fix: STILL REPRODUCES at services/flows/src/flows/executor.py:270 (and :268). The off-loop half is real (executor.py:169-182 `return await asyncio.to_thread(_regex, node, inputs)`), but the half the finding itself calls load-bearing is not. The audit's Fix reads: "cap the subject length, and EITHER reject nested-quantifier patterns OR move the match behind an `asyncio.wait_for` on a thread. A thread does not stop the GIL stall, so the length cap is the load-bearing half." The shipped cap is `_REGEX_MAX_SUBJECT = 256 * 1024` (executor.py:250), enforced at :260-261 -- 262144 chars. The documented attack is "one POST /api/flows/runs with a 40-character seed takes the flows pod out", which sails under that cap by four orders of magnitude. `grep -n 'wait_for|quantifier|re2' services/flows/src/flows/executor.py` finds no timeout and no pattern rejection: the only things between the caller's pattern and the engine are the length check and `compiled = re.compile(pattern)` (:263), then `compiled.finditer(payload.text)` (:270) / `compiled.sub(...)` (:268).  REPRODUCED against the current tree. A script driving the real `flows.executor.dispatch` with `FlowNode(kind='regex', config={'regexPattern': r'(a+)+$'})` and `Payload(text='a'*40 + '!')`, wrapped in `asyncio.wait_for(..., timeout=8)`, with a 50ms heartbeat task printing every second, output:      subject len 41 cap 262144 under cap: True     <nothing further; killed by `timeout 45`, exit=124>  Not one heartbeat tick in 45s of wall clock, and the 8-second `wait_for` never fired. That is the mechanism intact: `asyncio.to_thread` moves the call off the loop, but CPython's `re` does not release the GIL, so the worker thread freezes the event loop anyway -- including `/api/health`, the reason the finding says the liveness probe cannot save the pod.  The cited pinning test shows what was actually built: services/flows/tests/test_executor.py:472-477 asserts only that `_REGEX_MAX_SUBJECT + 1` chars raises "too large". Nothing asserts that a small subject with a catastrophic pattern terminates -- because it does not.]

**Current sites:** `services/flows/src/flows/executor.py:169-182`, `services/flows/src/flows/executor.py:250`, `services/flows/src/flows/executor.py:260`

**FIXED 2026-08-25 (Dapr sweep).** Closed by the engine swap, not by another cap: `_regex`
compiles and applies through the `regex` module with `timeout=2.0` on the `sub()` and `finditer()`
arms. The Dapr sweep re-derived this row and found the reasoning in the original remediation — and
in the shipped code comment — WRONG: the blow-up is exponential in the PATTERN, not the subject.
Measured against `(a+)+$`: `re` took **76.5 s at a thirty-character subject**, nowhere near the
256 KiB cap that was described as the load-bearing defence; `regex` answered in 0.0003 s at every n.
The cap stays, redescribed as the memory bound it actually is. Pinned by
`test_a_CATASTROPHIC_pattern_cannot_stall_the_process`. The full write-up was in the Dapr sweep's
file, deleted on completion of that backlog (2026-08-25); the commit that made this change carries it.

</details>

<details><summary><b>ING-01</b> [**PARTIAL**] — The ingest write door opens completely when APP_API_TOKEN is unset — and ingest is the only governed service with no startup guard closing that path <i>(ingest-domain, security, effort S)</i></summary>

**Sites:** `services/ingest/src/ingest/auth.py:116`, `services/ingest/src/ingest/auth.py:117`, `services/ingest/src/ingest/__init__.py:32`, `services/ingest/src/ingest/__init__.py:68`

**Why it matters.** auth.py's own module docstring states the stakes: "with `local-dir` it was one unauthenticated request to read the ingest pod's own filesystem into a governed table, and with `s3-prefix` it is an unauthenticated writer into any project's bronze tier." A blanked/rotated-away/typo'd `APP_API_TOKEN` in a Dapr-enabled deployment silently restores exactly that, and nothing reports it — the pod comes up healthy, `/api/health` is green, and the first symptom is data. medallion's identically-shaped `authorize_produce` has the same `if not expected: return`, but medallion's producer refuses to boot in that state; ingest does not, so the dev-open path is not actually confined to dev here.

**Fix.** Call `assert_app_token_configured(dapr_enabled=<the same flag the other five read>)` in `create_app()` (or at the head of `_lifespan`) so the pod crash-loops rather than serving an unauthenticated write door. Keep the dev-open branch — it is a recorded decision pinned by `test_ingest_auth.py:104` — but make it unreachable once Dapr ingest is on, and add the startup-guard test.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** Verified verbatim. auth.py:116-118 is `expected = os.environ.get("APP_API_TOKEN"); if not expected: return` — the entire gate. `grep -rn assert_app_token_configured services/ingest` returns nothing, while lineage/main.py:55, medallion/producer.py:57, medallion/mover.py:48, catalog/main.py:61 and maintenance/service.py:113 all call it; the helper exists at…


**Re-verified 2026-08-24 — PARTIAL (severity now low).** The full bypass is GONE. auth.py:123-136 now reads `expected = os.environ.get("APP_API_TOKEN")` … `if not expected and not (settings.oidc_enabled or settings.fga_enabled): return None  # dev: nothing configured to authenticate against`. Pinned by four new tests in services/ingest/tests/test_ingest_auth.py:285/291/305/316 (`test_an_unset_service_token_does_not_open_the_door_when_auth_is_ON`, `test_a_BLANK_service_token_...`, `test_the_local_dev_posture_is_preserved`). Fix commit cc01a6dc `fix(ingest): an absent service token is not an absent door (ING-01)`.  The SECOND half of the finding — the missing startup guard — is untouched: `grep -rn 'assert_app_token_configured' services/ingest/` returns NOTHING, while lineage/main.py:56, medallion/producer.py:61, medallion/mover.py:51, notifications/api/subscriptions.py:54, catalog/main.py:79 and maintenance/service.py:138 all call it. Since the audit, ingest GAINED a sidecar-delivered route it did not have: cron.py:130 `router.add_api_route(f"/{binding_name}", _on_tick, methods=["POST"])`, guarded only by `Depends(require_dapr_token)` (cron.py:69). dapr_auth.py:82 states the contract that guard depends on — "`assert_app_token_configured` makes that a startup error once Dapr ingest is actually enabled, so the no-op can only apply in dev" — and ingest never calls it. chart/values.yaml:139 declares `daprIngest: false` for ingest, so fleet.yaml:157-159 renders APP_API_TOKEN only via the `lanceWriter and auth.enabled` arm. With `--set auth.enabled=false` plus `services.ingest.cronBindingName` set, the tick route is live and unauthenticated. Severity dropped to low because auth.enabled defaults to TRUE (chart/values.yaml:706) and cronBindingName defaults to "".

**Current sites:** `services/ingest/src/ingest/auth.py:123`, `services/ingest/src/ingest/auth.py:135`, `services/ingest/src/ingest/cron.py:130`, `services/ingest/src/ingest/__init__.py:63`

</details>

<details><summary><b>MED-003</b> [**FIXED**] — The S3 secret key and the estate's APP_API_TOKEN are shipped into the Ray Jobs `runtime_env`, which the Jobs API echoes back to any reader <i>(medallion, security, effort M)</i></summary>

**Sites:** `services/medallion/src/medallion/services/ray_submit.py:68`, `services/medallion/src/medallion/services/ray_submit.py:159`, `services/medallion/src/medallion/services/ray_submit.py:163`

**Why it matters.** House rule 7 makes the Dapr/OpenBao secret store the SOLE source and fail-closed; that posture is undone at the last hop by copying the plaintext secret into a REST body that the Ray dashboard/Jobs API renders back to anyone who can `GET /api/jobs/<id>` (the same dashboard `services/compute` proxies at `/api/ray/*`). `APP_API_TOKEN` is the estate-wide service credential guarding every `require_dapr_token` route — a read of one job's runtime_env yields the key to the whole cascade, `/lineage-events` included.

**Fix.** Stop transporting credentials through `runtime_env`. Mount the S3 credentials and the lineage service token onto the Ray worker pods (k8s Secret / the same OpenBao-backed Dapr secret component the fleet already uses) and pass only the non-secret pointers (`FROM_URI`, `TO_URI`, `STAGE`, `MODEL`, `REGISTRY_URI`, OTEL config) in `runtime_env`. If the KubeRay merge is the intended point for this, gate the current path behind an explicit dev-only flag so a production render cannot take it.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** Exact quotes verified at ray_submit.py:68 (S3_SECRET via get_secret_value), :159 (LINEAGE_SERVICE_TOKEN from APP_API_TOKEN) and :163 (S3_SECRET again), all inside body['runtime_env']['env_vars'] POSTed to /api/jobs/ (:87-91, :185-188). The module's own comment at :156-158 concedes the Jobs API echoes runtime_env back and names the S3 credentials as having the same exposure. Not a sanctioned…


**Re-verified 2026-08-24 — OPEN (severity now high).** Stage path, inside `env_vars` that becomes `body["runtime_env"]["env_vars"]` (ray_submit.py:186) POSTed to /api/jobs/ (:204-205): line 112 `"S3_SECRET": settings.s3_secret_access_key.get_secret_value(),` and line 150 `"LINEAGE_SERVICE_TOKEN": os.environ.get("APP_API_TOKEN", ""),`. Train path, same shape (body at :238, runtime_env.env_vars at :241-292, POST at :303): line 268 `"LINEAGE_SERVICE_TOKEN": os.environ.get("APP_API_TOKEN", ""),` and line 272 `"S3_SECRET": settings.s3_secret_access_key.get_secret_value(),`. The module still concedes the exposure in its own comment at :265-267: "NOTE the token rides in the Ray runtime_env, which the Ray Jobs API echoes back — the SAME exposure the S3 credentials below already have. Tighten both together (a secret mounted on the Ray pods) at the KubeRay merge". Nothing changed on this axis. The only movement nearby is the `RASK_PARAM_` prefixing added at :136-139, whose comment explicitly notes it exists so "a lane cannot reach S3_SECRET" — i.e. the secret is acknowledged as present in that dict.

**Current sites:** `services/medallion/src/medallion/services/ray_submit.py:112`, `services/medallion/src/medallion/services/ray_submit.py:150`, `services/medallion/src/medallion/services/ray_submit.py:268`, `services/medallion/src/medallion/services/ray_submit.py:272`


**FIXED since `91d2e50d` + `49dc66cc` (2026-08-28).** No credential rides a job submission. `S3_SECRET` and `LINEAGE_SERVICE_TOKEN` reach the Ray pods by `secretKeyRef` (chart and the hand-applied head file both), and the submitter forwards coordinates only.

</details>

<details><summary><b>SK-01</b> [**FIXED**] — Write authorship comes from an unverified, client-supplied `X-User` header <i>(service-kit-core, security, effort S)</i></summary>

**Sites:** `packages/service-kit/src/service_kit/media/deps.py:29`, `services/annotator/src/annotator/annotations/save.py:58`, `services/annotator/src/annotator/annotations/tags.py:95`

**Why it matters.** Any caller who can reach the annotator can attribute an annotation write to an arbitrary principal by setting one header. In a national-archive system the author stamp is the provenance record; a forgeable one silently corrupts the audit trail and any downstream review/attribution query, and it does so invisibly (the write succeeds, FGA passes, nothing logs an anomaly). The verified subject the service already computes makes this a wiring gap, not a missing capability.

**Fix.** Make the write paths depend on the verified subject: in the annotator, replace `author: AuthorDep` with `author: CurrentSubject` on `save_annotations` and the tags route. In service-kit, either delete `get_author`/`AuthorDep` or narrow it to read-only surfaces and rename it so the trust level is in the name (e.g. `UntrustedAuthorDep`); better, move the seam into `service_kit.governed` so the shared kit exposes exactly one identity dep and the header path cannot be reached from a governed service. If the header must survive for dev, gate it on an explicit `auth disabled` setting rather than on its own absence.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** Verified all three sites verbatim. `packages/service-kit/src/service_kit/media/deps.py:29-37` defines `get_author(x_user: Annotated[str|None, Header(alias="X-User")] = None)` returning `(x_user or "").strip() or "anon"`, exported as `AuthorDep`. `services/annotator/src/annotator/annotations/save.py:58` and `tags.py:95` both take `author: AuthorDep`; save.py:100-108 stamps `fields["reviewer"] =…


**Re-verified 2026-08-24 — OPEN (severity now high).** packages/service-kit/src/service_kit/media/deps.py:29-37 verbatim: `def get_author(x_user: Annotated[str | None, Header(alias="X-User")] = None) -> str:` … `return (x_user or "").strip() or "anon"` / `AuthorDep = Annotated[str, Depends(get_author)]`. Both write routes still consume it: save.py:58 `author: AuthorDep,` and its stamping at save.py:105-108 `fields["reviewer"] = author` plus the insert identity `"reviewer": author`; tags.py:95 `author: AuthorDep,` feeding tags.py:113 `delta = tag_rows(body.adds, declared, author=author, …)`. `grep -rn 'X-User|AuthorDep|get_author' services/ packages/` returns no replacement by a verified subject — annotator/api/security.py:13 still documents the header path as live. Unchanged since the audit base. The verified-subject dep exists in the same service (annotator/api/security.py) but is not wired into either write path. services/viewer/api/dependencies.py:7 re-exports AuthorDep as well, so the forgeable seam is exported to a third service.

**Current sites:** `packages/service-kit/src/service_kit/media/deps.py:29`, `services/annotator/src/annotator/annotations/save.py:58`, `services/annotator/src/annotator/annotations/tags.py:95`


**FIXED since `1e0a760e` (2026-08-28).** Write authorship is the VERIFIED subject now: `service_kit.media.deps` resolves it from the bearer and the gateway strips a client-supplied `X-User` at the edge, so the header can no longer name anyone.

</details>

<details><summary><b>VS-03</b> [**FIXED**] — 25 of the viewer's 32 routes serve corpus content with no authn and no FGA gate, including every media-byte route <i>(viewer-search, security, effort L)</i></summary>

**Sites:** `services/viewer/src/viewer/api/v1/endpoints/media.py:283`, `services/viewer/src/viewer/api/v1/endpoints/media.py:161`, `services/viewer/src/viewer/api/v1/endpoints/media.py:184`, `services/viewer/src/viewer/api/v1/endpoints/media.py:242`, `services/viewer/src/viewer/api/v1/endpoints/transcripts.py:41` *(+5 more)*

**Why it matters.** The service's own `security.py` docstring is explicit that the untrusted-network posture "stops being defensible the moment more than one person can reach the zone," and that a corpus LIST alone is sensitive. Yet the list is gated while the bytes, the transcripts, the entity graph and the atlas selection rows behind it are not — anyone who can reach `:8101` (or `/api/explorer/*` through the gateway) reads every corpus in full. A gate applied to the index but not the content is not a partial control; it is a control that can be bypassed by guessing one `doc_id`.

**Fix.** Decide and apply one stance per route family. At minimum, put the byte-serving routes (`/api/media`, `/api/thumbnail`, `/api/chunk-frame`, `/api/media-clip`) behind `READ_DATA` on `corpus_object(settings, dataset_id, row_table)` and the content-listing routes (transcripts, atlas chunks, graph, voice, documents) behind `READ_METADATA`, reusing the existing `CheckerDep`/`CurrentSubject` deps — applied at the `APIRouter(dependencies=[...])` level so a new route cannot be added ungated. `POST /api/graph/cypher` needs a stronger gate than the read routes, or removal from the deployed surface.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** Counted directly: 32 `@router.<verb>` decorators across services/viewer/src/viewer/api/v1/endpoints/; CheckerDep appears only in datasets.py (2 routes), objects.py (3) and pages.py (2) = 7 gated, 25 ungated. Spot-checked the cited ungated sites: media.py:283 `def media(...)` streams the media blob with no subject/checker; media.py:161 thumbnail, 184 chunk-frame, 242 media-clip likewise;…


**Re-verified 2026-08-24 — OPEN (severity now high).** Recounted at HEAD: 32 `@router.<verb>` decorators across services/viewer/src/viewer/api/v1/endpoints/ (atlas 5, chunks 2, datasets 2, diarization 1, graph 5, media 4, objects 3, pages 2, system 3, topics 1, voice 4). `grep -n 'CheckerDep|CurrentSubject'` across the same directory returns hits in exactly three files — datasets.py:23 (2 routes), objects.py:41 (3 routes), pages.py:34 (2 routes) = 7 gated, 25 ungated. `grep -n 'dependencies='` over the directory and over api/v1/router.py returns NOTHING, so there is no router-level gate either: router.py:22-36 is a bare `router.include_router(r)` loop. Every media-byte route is still open: media.py:306 `def media(doc_id: str, request: Request, state: StateDep, dataset: DatasetParam = None) -> Response:`, :174 `def thumbnail(...)`, :204 `def chunk_frame(...)`, :265 `def media_clip(...)` — none takes a subject or a checker. `POST /api/graph/cypher` (graph.py:327-328) is likewise ungated. security.py:1-7 still carries the docstring the audit quoted. transcripts.py:41 (an original cited site) no longer exists — those routes were renamed away in c22c00d2 — but the count and the stance are unchanged.

**Current sites:** `services/viewer/src/viewer/api/v1/endpoints/media.py:306`, `services/viewer/src/viewer/api/v1/endpoints/media.py:174`, `services/viewer/src/viewer/api/v1/endpoints/media.py:204`, `services/viewer/src/viewer/api/v1/endpoints/media.py:265`, `services/viewer/src/viewer/api/v1/endpoints/graph.py:328`, `services/viewer/src/viewer/api/v1/endpoints/atlas.py:80`, `services/viewer/src/viewer/api/v1/endpoints/chunks.py:41`, `services/viewer/src/viewer/api/v1/endpoints/voice.py:74`, `services/viewer/src/viewer/api/v1/endpoints/system.py:150`, `services/viewer/src/viewer/api/v1/endpoints/topics.py:47`, `services/viewer/src/viewer/api/v1/endpoints/diarization.py:45`


**FIXED since `715f73e3` (2026-08-28).** All 24 corpus routes are gated. `viewer/api/security.py` grew `_corpus_gate(relation)` / `_media_bytes_gate()` — `can_get_metadata` for a listing, `can_read_data` for payload and bytes — wired as router `dependencies=[...]`, and a route-walk test descends `original_router` (the earlier walk read `app.routes`, which in this FastAPI version holds only `_IncludedRouter` wrappers, so it passed on an app with 24 open routes).

</details>

<details><summary><b>X6</b> [**FIXED**] — `search` is the only explorer service with no authn/authz code path at all — the chart's estate-wide OIDC/FGA env has nothing to bind to <i>(cross-service, security, effort M)</i></summary>

**Sites:** `services/search/src/search/core/config.py:15`, `services/search/src/search/main.py:56`, `services/search/src/search/api/v1/router.py:43`, `chart/templates/explorer.yaml:140`, `services/viewer/src/viewer/api/security.py:1`

**Why it matters.** The chart comment's premise — 'a service with no such route is unaffected' — is exactly the hole for search: on an `auth.enabled` estate, viewer gates corpus metadata and bytes on `can_get_metadata`/`can_read_data` and annotator gates its task plane, while search returns actual search HITS (row content, transcript text, filter facets) from the same corpora to any caller that can reach the gateway. `viewer/api/security.py:1` documents this precise posture as the defect it was written to close ('A corpus LIST is itself sensitive: it names data someone may not know exists') — search still has the pre-fix state, and unlike a misconfiguration it cannot be closed by any values change.

**Fix.** Give search the same door its two siblings already have and share: change `SearchSettings` to inherit `GovernedAuthSettings`, add `search/api/security.py` built on `service_kit.governed.deps.make_auth_deps` (the module viewer already uses — no new mechanism), and gate the search router on `can_read_data` for the resolved target table via a router-level `dependencies=[...]` so no endpoint can be added ungated. Add a unit test asserting all three explorer apps refuse an unauthenticated request when `LANCE_OIDC_ENABLED=true`.


**Re-verified 2026-08-24 — OPEN (severity now high).** `find services/search/src -name '*.py'` shows NO `api/security.py` and no auth module of any kind. services/search/src/search/core/config.py:14 `class SearchSettings(Settings):` — still no `GovernedAuthSettings` mixin, in contrast to services/viewer/src/viewer/core/config.py:16 `class ViewerSettings(Settings, GovernedAuthSettings):` and services/annotator/src/annotator/core/config.py:22 `class AnnotatorSettings(GovernedAuthSettings, Settings):`. services/search/src/search/main.py:33-53 lifespan builds no OIDCVerifier and no FGA client. services/search/src/search/api/v1/router.py:46 `router = APIRouter(prefix="/api", tags=["search"])` — no `dependencies=[...]`. The chart premise the audit quoted is still in the tree: chart/templates/_helpers.tpl:1056 'Safe to set estate-wide: these change behaviour ONLY where a route declares an auth dependency, so a service with no gated route is unaffected'. Unchanged in every particular since the base commit. search remains the only explorer service with no authn/authz code path.

**Current sites:** `services/search/src/search/core/config.py:14`, `services/search/src/search/main.py:33`, `services/search/src/search/api/v1/router.py:46`, `chart/templates/_helpers.tpl:1056`


**FIXED since `3593381c` (2026-08-28).** `SearchSettings(Settings, GovernedAuthSettings)` binds the estate's OIDC/FGA env, and `search/api/security.py` gates every route on `can_read_data` for the corpus's search ROW table — refused for one named corpus, FILTERED for a fan-out.

</details>

<details><summary><b>catalog-api-02</b> [**FIXED**] — GET /v1/stores and /v1/stores/tiers disclose the whole estate's buckets and hosts with no authorization gate, while the sibling POST calls that same… <i>(catalog-api, security, effort S)</i></summary>

**Sites:** `services/catalog/src/catalog/api/v1/endpoints/stores.py:63`, `services/catalog/src/catalog/api/v1/endpoints/stores.py:116`, `services/catalog/src/catalog/api/v1/endpoints/stores.py:96`, `services/catalog/src/catalog/api/fga_deps.py:408`, `services/catalog/src/catalog/api/fga_deps.py:417`

**Why it matters.** The read of an estate-wide fact is ungated while the write of the same fact is estate-admin gated — the asymmetry means any authenticated principal (any project member) enumerates every object store the estate knows, including `endpoint` hosts of attached third-party buckets. The three comparable estate-wide surfaces all gate or filter: `/v1/events` (`require_relation` can_observe_events), `/v1/projects` (same), `/v1/warehouses` (`fga.list_objects` filter). This is the systemic hazard of the `{id}`-keyed router guard: ~30 routes whose path param is not literally `id` (`{warehouse_id}`, `{project_id}`, `{model}`) or which have none at all rely entirely on a hand-written in-handler gate, and these two were written without one.

**Fix.** Gate both reads on `can_observe_events` against `settings.fga_root_object` exactly as `attach_store` does (or FGA-filter per store if a narrower tier is wanted), and add a test that an authenticated non-estate-admin gets 403 from `GET /v1/stores`. Separately, add a contract test that enumerates every mounted route and asserts each one either matches a `_RESOURCES` prefix (router-gated) or names an explicit `require_relation`/`require_can_*` call — so the next ungated estate surface fails a test rather than shipping.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** Verified. stores.py:63-66 `list_stores(state: UserStateStoreDep)` and :121-132 `stores_by_tier(state: UserStateStoreDep)` take no token/client and call no require_relation, while the write sibling at stores.py:96 does `require_relation(..., relation="can_observe_events", obj=settings.fga_root_object)`. The router-level guard cannot cover them: api/v1/router.py mounts every module under…


**Re-verified 2026-08-24 — OPEN (severity now high).** stores.py:79-82 `@router.get("/stores", ...)` / `async def list_stores(state: UserStateStoreDep) -> StoreRegistry:` — no token, no client, no require_relation. stores.py:149-154 `@router.get("/stores/tiers", ...)` / `async def stores_by_tier(state: UserStateStoreDep)` — same. The write sibling still gates: stores.py:112 `await fga_deps.require_relation(client, settings, token, relation="can_observe_events", obj=settings.fga_root_object)`. The router guard still cannot reach them: fga_deps.py:66 `_RESOURCES: tuple[str, ...] = ("namespace", "table", "materialized_view", "transaction")` and :206-212 `_resource_for` returns None for any path not `/v1/<one of those>/`, while stores.py:40 mounts `APIRouter(prefix="/v1")`. No route-enumeration contract test was added: `ls tests/unit | grep -i store` returns nothing. Untouched by any of the 46 commits in this window except the concurrency rewrite of the POST sibling — the asymmetry the finding names is unchanged, and the suggested contract test does not exist.

**Current sites:** `services/catalog/src/catalog/api/v1/endpoints/stores.py:79`, `services/catalog/src/catalog/api/v1/endpoints/stores.py:149`, `services/catalog/src/catalog/api/v1/endpoints/stores.py:112`, `services/catalog/src/catalog/api/fga_deps.py:206`, `services/catalog/src/catalog/api/v1/router.py:40`

<details><summary><b>ratch-003</b> [**DISSOLVED**] — Every `AWS_*` env var — including the secret access key — is copied into the Ray Job's runtime_env <i>(ratch, security, effort M)</i></summary>

**Sites:** `packages/ratch/src/ratch/core/jobs.py:47`, `packages/ratch/src/ratch/core/jobs.py:160`, `packages/ratch/src/ratch/core/jobs.py:164`

**Why it matters.** `AWS_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY` and `AWS_SESSION_TOKEN` match that prefix and land verbatim in the job's `runtime_env.env_vars`. Ray persists the submitted runtime_env in the job record and serves it back through the dashboard's job API (`GET /api/jobs/<submission_id>`) and the Jobs UI — and `JobsSettings.ray_address` defaults to `http://127.0.0.1:8265` (:70), the unauthenticated Ray dashboard this repo runs (CLAUDE.md: "No auth, no app middleware. The services assume localhost / trusted network"). So a long-lived credential is written into a queryable, plaintext, persisted control-plane record every time a runner is dispatched. This is precisely the env-var secret propagation HOUSE-RULE-7 forbids: the Dapr/OpenBao secret store is the sole sanctioned source, fail-closed, and the memory note is explicit — "OpenBao via Dapr is the SOLE source, fail-closed; no env fallback".

**Fix.** Stop forwarding a credential-bearing prefix wholesale. Narrow `_FORWARDED_ENV_PREFIXES` to non-secret configuration only (`MEDIA_`, plus explicitly named non-secret S3 knobs like `RASK_S3_ENDPOINT_URL`/`AWS_REGION`/`AWS_DEFAULT_REGION` via an allow-list of exact names, not a prefix), and have the runner obtain its store credentials the same way the fleet does — from the Dapr secret store at worker start, failing closed if absent. If the local dev loop genuinely needs creds on the worker, that belongs in the cluster's own secret material (a k8s Secret mounted into the Ray worker pod), never in a per-job submission body.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** Verbatim: jobs.py:47 `_FORWARDED_ENV_PREFIXES = ('MEDIA_', 'AWS_')`; :160 `forwarded = {k: v for k, v in os.environ.items() if k.startswith(_FORWARDED_ENV_PREFIXES)}`; :164 returns it as `env_vars`, and `_submit_or_resubmit` passes that dict as the job body's `runtime_env` (:176-180). `AWS_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY`/`AWS_SESSION_TOKEN` match the prefix, and Ray persists+serves runtime_env.env_vars…


**Re-verified 2026-08-24 — OPEN (severity now high).** core/jobs.py:47 `_FORWARDED_ENV_PREFIXES = ("MEDIA_", "AWS_")` — unchanged. core/jobs.py:160 `forwarded = {k: v for k, v in os.environ.items() if k.startswith(_FORWARDED_ENV_PREFIXES)}`, returned at :164 as `"env_vars": forwarded` inside `_job_runtime_env`, which `_submit_or_resubmit` passes as the job body's `"runtime_env": _job_runtime_env(job)` (:175). `AWS_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY` / `AWS_SESSION_TOKEN` still match the prefix. No allow-list, no Dapr/OpenBao lookup — `grep -rn 'tenacity\|secret\|dapr' packages/ratch/src/` finds no secret-store path anywhere in the package.

**Current sites:** `/home/blackwell/Desktop/rask/packages/ratch/src/ratch/core/jobs.py:47`, `/home/blackwell/Desktop/rask/packages/ratch/src/ratch/core/jobs.py:160`, `/home/blackwell/Desktop/rask/packages/ratch/src/ratch/core/jobs.py:164`


**FIXED since `9205f783` (2026-08-28), HARDENED same day.** The forward is now FAIL-CLOSED: a name rides the submission only when its shape says coordinate (`_SAFE_NAME_SUFFIXES`), with the credential markers kept as a second veto. The first fix was a denylist, and the ratch/ray-kit review (open_ray-kernel.md, move 1) measured its failure mode: `MEDIA_API_KEY` matches no marker and walked straight through into the echoing Jobs API. The property both versions defend is unchanged — a new `MEDIA_*_URL` needs no platform edit — but the default for a shape nobody has heard of flipped from shipped to withheld.

</details>

#### The high-severity items in this epic

<details><summary><b>ANN-02</b> [**FIXED**] — Publish saga is fire-and-forget with a module-global in-flight set — a lost task strands the project in `publishing` forever <i>(annotator, resilience, effort M)</i></summary>

**Sites:** `services/annotator/src/annotator/projects/lakehouse.py:277`, `services/annotator/src/annotator/projects/lakehouse.py:280`, `services/annotator/src/annotator/projects/lakehouse.py:307`, `services/annotator/src/annotator/projects/project_actor.py:433`

**Why it matters.** HOUSE-RULE-15 (create_task without holding a reference — asyncio keeps only a weak reference, so a suspended task can be GC'd) and HOUSE-RULE-11 (module globals holding mutable state). The failure is not merely a lost run: if `_drive` is collected or cancelled without its `finally` completing, `_RUNNING` retains the project id, every subsequent 60 s watchdog tick logs "already running — the tick stands down" (`:283`), and `PROJECT_EDGES` (machines.py:38-39) has **no principal-fireable edge out of `PUBLISHING`** — `SYSTEM_ONLY_EVENTS` refuses `publish_succeeded`/`publish_failed` over HTTP (project_events.py:184). The project is unrecoverable without a pod restart, which is exactly the stranding the watchdog reminder exists to prevent.

**Fix.** Hold a strong reference (`app.state`-scoped `set[asyncio.Task]` with `task.add_done_callback(tasks.discard)`), and make `_RUNNING` an attribute of an object owned by the lifespan rather than a module global. Better: make the guard self-healing — store a monotonic start timestamp instead of a bare id and let a tick re-drive once the entry is older than one period, so a lost task costs one extra tick rather than the project.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** Verified line-for-line. lakehouse.py:277 `_RUNNING: set[str] = set()`; :280-285 guard + add; :304-305 `finally: _RUNNING.discard(...)`; :307 `return asyncio.get_running_loop().create_task(_drive())`. Sole caller project_actor.py:433 `lakehouse.spawn_publish(project.project_id)` discards the Task — no strong reference anywhere. The stranding chain checks out: machines.py:37-38 give PUBLISHING…


**Re-verified 2026-08-24 — OPEN (severity now high).** Mechanism unchanged, only line-shifted (+17). lakehouse.py:294 `_RUNNING: set[str] = set()` — still a module global. lakehouse.py:299-302 `    if project_id in _RUNNING:` / `        logger.debug("publish for %s already running — the tick stands down", project_id)` / `        return None` / `    _RUNNING.add(project_id)`. lakehouse.py:322 `            _RUNNING.discard(project_id)` in the `finally`. lakehouse.py:324 `    return asyncio.get_running_loop().create_task(_drive())` — no strong reference held anywhere; `grep -rn 'add_done_callback\|_tasks\|task_set' services/annotator/src/annotator/` returns nothing. Sole caller project_actor.py:451 `        lakehouse.spawn_publish(project.project_id)` discards the returned Task. The stranding chain is intact: machines.py:38-39 `(ProjectState.PUBLISHING, "publish_succeeded")` and `(ProjectState.PUBLISHING, "publish_failed")` both carry permission `None`; project_events.py:67 `SYSTEM_ONLY_EVENTS: Final[frozenset[str]] = frozenset(e for (_s, e), (_t, p) in PROJECT_EDGES.items() if p is None)` and :203 `    if payload.event in SYSTEM_ONLY_EVENTS:` refuses them over HTTP — so PUBLISHING still has no principal-fireable exit. Nothing in the 443-file churn touched spawn_publish; git diff 0ea0b7c2..HEAD -- services/annotator/src/annotator/projects/lakehouse.py shows the only changes in that file are the publish-secret bundle (:104) and tag-convergence text.

**Current sites:** `services/annotator/src/annotator/projects/lakehouse.py:294`, `services/annotator/src/annotator/projects/lakehouse.py:299`, `services/annotator/src/annotator/projects/lakehouse.py:324`, `services/annotator/src/annotator/projects/project_actor.py:451`


**FIXED by `54fb45ea` (2026-08-26)** — two days after the last re-verify, which is why the row still read OPEN. `lakehouse.py:305` holds `_TASKS`, `:344` is `task.add_done_callback(_TASKS.discard)`, and `_RUNNING.add` moved to `:338` (after `create_task`), closing a second leak the finding never named. `tests/unit/test_publish_spawn_does_not_leak.py` passes today. The re-verify's own grep evidence (`add_done_callback` 'returns nothing') is falsified by `lakehouse.py:344`. The residual — module-global `_RUNNING`/`_TASKS` vs HOUSE-RULE-11 — is deliberately left: one app per process, Dapr single-placement makes process-local correct, and `spawn_publish` is called from an actor reminder with no `app` handle.

</details>

<details><summary><b>CAT-CORE-01</b> [**FIXED**] — Commit idempotency guard fails OPEN on any storage error, re-enabling the duplicate-append it exists to prevent <i>(catalog-core, error-handling, effort S)</i></summary>

**Sites:** `services/catalog/src/catalog/services/dataplane.py:621`, `services/catalog/src/catalog/services/dataplane.py:623`, `services/catalog/src/catalog/services/dataplane.py:629`, `services/catalog/src/catalog/services/dataplane.py:632`, `services/catalog/src/catalog/services/dataplane.py:675`

**Why it matters.** This is the one guard standing between a Dapr activity replay and duplicated bronze rows; `services/catalog/tests/test_commit_idempotency.py:9-10` records that the sibling hole was measured at "nine copies per file". Append never conflicts with Append, so nothing downstream refuses the duplicate — a single transient read failure during a retry silently corrupts the table's row count. The per-version `continue` has the same shape: a transient failure reading OUR OWN marker version skips it and the replay appends again.

**Fix.** Discriminate: treat only a genuinely-absent dataset (`FileNotFoundError` / lance's "was not found" message shape, the discrimination `read_blob` already performs at dataplane.py:804-815) as "no prior commit"; let every other exception propagate as `ServiceUnavailableError` so the caller retries rather than duplicating. Same for the per-version read: skip only on a missing/GC'd transaction file, propagate on a store error. Add a test that injects a raising storage layer and asserts the commit is refused, not duplicated.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** Verified at dataplane.py:620-637. `_find_run_commit` has exactly the two blanket `except Exception` handlers cited: line 623 returns None on ANY failure of `lance.dataset(location, ...)` with the comment "no dataset yet -> certainly no prior commit by this run", and line 632 `continue`s past ANY failure of `read_transaction(version)`. `commit_appended_fragments` (line 675-678) treats `None` as…


**Re-verified 2026-08-24 — OPEN (severity now high).** dataplane.py:621-633 verbatim at HEAD: 621	    try: 622	        dataset = lance.dataset(location, storage_options=dict(so) if so else None) 623	    except Exception: 624	        return None  # no dataset yet -> certainly no prior commit by this run ... 629	        try: 630	            transaction = dataset.read_transaction(version) 631	            props = getattr(transaction, "transaction_properties", None) or {} 632	        except Exception: 633	            continue Both blanket handlers are unchanged. commit_appended_fragments still treats None as 'no prior commit' and appends: dataplane.py:676 `already = _find_run_commit(location, so, run_id, read_version)` / :677-678 `if already is not None: return already`. No FileNotFoundError/'was not found' discrimination was added. The fix's second half is also missing: `grep -rn 'fail.open\|ServiceUnavailable\|raising' services/catalog/tests/test_commit_idempotency.py` returns nothing, and the file's five tests are the same five (test_a_replayed_commit_..., test_DIFFERENT_runs_..., test_a_commit_WITHOUT_a_run_id_..., test_the_replay_check_looks_only_AFTER_read_version, test_an_interleaved_FOREIGN_commit_...) — none injects a raising storage layer. Line drift only (+2). `git diff 0ea0b7c2..HEAD -- services/.../dataplane.py` shows 92 changed lines, none in _find_run_commit. Note the interaction with CAT-CORE-03: the tests now DO run in CI, but they never covered this failure mode, so closing 03 bought no coverage for 01.

**Current sites:** `services/catalog/src/catalog/services/dataplane.py:623`, `services/catalog/src/catalog/services/dataplane.py:632`, `services/catalog/src/catalog/services/dataplane.py:676`


**FIXED since `f28db891` (2026-08-28).** `_find_run_commit` fails CLOSED: only a recognised ABSENCE (`_is_absence`) is treated as 'no prior commit'; any other store error raises `ServiceUnavailableError` instead of re-enabling the duplicate append.

</details>

<details><summary><b>MED-002</b> [**PARTIAL**] — The in-process HTR lane holds the Dapr ack — and a process-wide asyncio.Lock — across the entire multi-page GPU transcription <i>(medallion, resilience, effort L)</i></summary>

**Sites:** `services/medallion/src/medallion/services/transform.py:225`, `services/medallion/src/medallion/services/transform.py:256`, `services/medallion/src/medallion/services/htr_stage.py:82`, `services/medallion/src/medallion/core/config.py:174`, `services/medallion/src/medallion/services/ray_submit.py:103`

**Why it matters.** ackWait expires long before the stage returns, so the sidecar redelivers; each redelivery enters `handle_stage`, blocks on `_write_lock`, and pins a Starlette threadpool worker (default 40) while waiting. When the lock finally releases the redelivery re-reads every page and re-runs every GPU inference from scratch — N× the compute cost per redelivery, up to `maxDeliver`, then the DLQ. The A13 rework removed exactly this shape from the Ray stage path but left it on the in-process/HTR path, which `transform.py:249-254` dispatches to FIRST, ahead of the Ray branch.

**Fix.** Give the HTR lane the same submit-and-ack shape the Ray stage path already has: dispatch the transcription as a job (or an internal durable task) and return SUCCESS immediately, letting the registered commit + publication event wake the next tier. Until P7b lands, at minimum bound the handler (`asyncio.timeout` well inside ackWait, returning RETRY) and make `_write_lock` acquisition non-blocking (`if _write_lock.locked(): return _RETRY`) so redeliveries do not queue up holding workers.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** Verified: transform.py:225 `async with _write_lock:` wraps the whole compute, :249-270 dispatches the HTR lane FIRST (before the ray branch), htr_stage.py:82-89 loops every bronze page with a blocking httpx call bounded by htrflow_timeout_seconds (config.py:174, default 600.0), and ray_submit.py:103-106 states the ack rule this breaks. The lane is live by default, not a toggle: chart…


**Re-verified 2026-08-24 — PARTIAL (severity now low).** The HTR half is gone: commit 95bc522d "refactor(medallion)!: the platform has no concept of a workload" deleted services/medallion/src/medallion/services/htr_stage.py (163 lines), htr_transcribe.py (49) and htr_parse.py (202); `ls services/medallion/src/medallion/services/ | grep -i htr` returns nothing, and `grep -rn 'htrflow' services/medallion/src/` returns nothing (config.py:174's `htrflow_timeout_seconds` is gone with it). The Ray branch now dispatches and acks in milliseconds — transform.py:545-556 `instance_id = _dispatch_stage_workflow(...)` then :559 `return _SUCCESS`. RESIDUAL: the process-wide lock is unchanged and still acquired BLOCKING across the whole in-process compute — transform.py:72 `_write_lock = asyncio.Lock()` and transform.py:479 `async with _write_lock:` wrapping read_upstream / ensure_stage_output / transform_stage / the quality read. The audit's suggested mitigation (`if _write_lock.locked(): return _RETRY`) was not applied, so a redelivery still queues on the lock rather than returning RETRY. The cited GPU-transcription mechanism is deleted; what remains is the fake-Ray in-process Lance write (small, overwrite-idempotent), so the ack-window exposure is a fraction of what was filed. Severity dropped HIGH -> low.

**Current sites:** `services/medallion/src/medallion/services/transform.py:72`, `services/medallion/src/medallion/services/transform.py:479`

</details>

<details><summary><b>ingest-flow-02</b> [**FIXED**] — Three of four NATS connect sites have no timeout, against the file's own measured evidence that a connect to a dead broker never returns <i>(ingest-flow, resilience, effort S)</i></summary>

**Sites:** `services/ingest/src/ingest/runtime.py:192`, `services/ingest/src/ingest/runtime.py:232`, `services/ingest/src/ingest/runtime.py:291`, `services/ingest/src/ingest/queue.py:127`

**Why it matters.** These three sites are the bodies of the `publish_units`, `drain_chunk` and `reconcile_chunk` ACTIVITIES. A Dapr activity has no execution timeout, so an unreachable or slow-to-DNS broker hangs the activity indefinitely: `ACTIVITY_RETRY` never fires (nothing raised), the child workflow never returns, the parent's `when_all` never completes, and the run sits RUNNING forever with no error anywhere. The one place a bound exists is the tidy-up path, which is the least important of the four. Resilience guidance is "timeouts everywhere — every network call"; here the plane measured the failure, wrote the fix, and applied it to one call.

**Fix.** Put the bound inside the seam: give `WorkQueue.connect` an explicit `connect_timeout` / `max_reconnect_attempts` and wrap it in `asyncio.wait_for` there, so every caller inherits it and no future call site can forget. Give the drain a separate, larger overall deadline (it legitimately runs long) but keep the CONNECT bounded at seconds. Add a test that points `RASK_NATS_URL` at a black-hole address and asserts each activity raises within the bound rather than hanging.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** Verified byte-for-byte. runtime.py:192, :232, :291 are each `queue = await WorkQueue.connect(nats_url())` with no bound, inside `publish_chunk_units`, `drain_chunk_units` and `reconcile_from_queue` — the bodies of the `publish_units`, `drain_chunk` and `reconcile_chunk` activities respectively. queue.py:127-128 is `async def connect(cls, servers, **options) -> Self: nc = await…


**Re-verified 2026-08-24 — OPEN (severity now high).** The seam is still unbounded: queue.py:151-153 `async def connect(cls, servers: str | list[str], **options: Any) -> Self:` / `nc = await nats.connect(servers, **options)` — no connect_timeout, no max_reconnect_attempts default. Three of the four call sites still pass no options: runtime.py:227 `queue = await WorkQueue.connect(nats_url())` (in `publish_chunk_units`, the `publish_units` activity body), runtime.py:286 same line (in `drain_chunk_units`, the `drain_chunk` activity), runtime.py:363 same line (in `reconcile_from_queue`, the `reconcile_chunk` activity). Only the tidy-up path is bounded — runtime.py:343 `queue = await asyncio.wait_for(WorkQueue.connect(nats_url()), timeout=RELEASE_TIMEOUT_SECONDS)`. `grep -n 'wait_for\|timeout' services/ingest/src/ingest/workflow.py` returns NOTHING, so no wrapping exists one layer up either. Unchanged in substance. The plane now states the measurement in two more places (runtime.py:319-321 and queue.py:429-431: 'a connect to a dead address ... had still not returned after 60 seconds') and applied wait_for to a FIFTH site (`inspect_queue`, queue.py:437) — so four call sites now document the hazard and two guard against it, while the three activity bodies that can wedge a run forever still do not.

**Current sites:** `services/ingest/src/ingest/runtime.py:227`, `services/ingest/src/ingest/runtime.py:286`, `services/ingest/src/ingest/runtime.py:363`, `services/ingest/src/ingest/queue.py:151`


**FIXED (2026-08-28).** The bound moved INTO the seam: `WorkQueue.connect` wraps its own `nats.connect` in `asyncio.wait_for(CONNECT_TIMEOUT_SECONDS)` and raises `BrokerUnreachable`, so all three activity bodies inherit it and a fourth call site cannot forget. `asyncio.wait_for` and not the client's `connect_timeout`, for the reason the plane had already measured twice.

<details><summary><b>ingest-flow-04</b> [**PARTIAL**] — The run-deadline path abandons its child workflows and then purges the queue underneath them <i>(ingest-flow, dapr-events, effort M)</i></summary>

**Sites:** `services/ingest/src/ingest/workflow.py:295`, `services/ingest/src/ingest/workflow.py:296`, `services/ingest/src/ingest/workflow.py:306`, `services/ingest/src/ingest/workflow.py:522`, `services/ingest/src/ingest/queue.py:273`

**Why it matters.** On the deadline path the parent declares the run FAILED while every unfinished `chunk_run` child is still durably executing — each still inside `drain_chunk`, fetching from the source and writing Lance fragments against `dataset_uri`. `release_run_units` then purges the run's subject and deletes the shared durable consumer out from under those live pull subscriptions, so in-flight acks and fetches fail against a consumer that no longer exists. The fragments those children already staged are never collected: `purge_staged` runs only inside `finalize_run`, which the timeout path deliberately skips. The net state is orphaned children still hammering a rate-limited source for a run nobody will commit, plus staged bytes with no owner — which is precisely the "unreclaimable orphan" condition `release_run` was written to prevent, reintroduced one layer up. The comment at :299-301 claims "the…

**Fix.** On the deadline (and any early-terminal) path, terminate the outstanding child workflow instances before emitting terminal — `ctx` cannot do it, so pass the child instance ids out to a `terminate_children` activity that uses `DaprWorkflowClient.terminate_workflow`, and only release the queue once they are quiescent. At minimum, make `release_run_units` a no-op when children may still be live, and make `purge_staged(uri, run_id)` run on every terminal path (not only inside `finalize_run`) so abandoned fragments are reclaimed rather than orphaned.

Nothing anywhere terminates the outstanding `chunk_run` children — grep for `terminate_workflow` in the plane returns nothing. `emit_terminal`…


**Re-verified 2026-08-24 — PARTIAL (severity now low).** A `terminate_chunks` activity now runs BEFORE `emit_terminal` on both early-terminal paths. Deadline path, workflow.py:652-658: `# STOP THE CHILDREN BEFORE RECLAIMING THEIR QUEUE. ...` / `yield ctx.call_activity(terminate_chunks, input={"child_ids": child_ids}, retry_policy=ACTIVITY_RETRY)` / `yield ctx.call_activity(emit_terminal, ...)`. Error-boundary path, workflow.py:722-724: `if child_ids:` / `yield ctx.call_activity(terminate_chunks, ...)` / `yield ctx.call_activity(emit_terminal, ...)`. The activity itself, workflow.py:1229-1231: `for child_id in child_ids:` / `client.terminate_workflow(child_id)`. `child_ids` is replay-safe by construction — workflow.py:619 `child_ids = [f"{spec.run_id}-c{i}" for i in range(len(chunks))]`, derived from `chunks`, an activity result. Commit ca758804 ('nothing could stop a live run — DWF-MGT-003') plus 4aa97c5b ('stop the fan-out before reclaiming its queue — §2.4, which a fix had made worse'). The Appendix-A unfiled extra at old workflow.py:325 (the `except Exception` branch having the identical defect, unconditionally on the default config) is closed by the SAME change at workflow.py:722-723 and is explicitly named in the comment there. RESIDUAL, declined by design: the audit's 'at minimum' also asked for `purge_staged` on every terminal path. It still runs only inside `finalize_run` (runtime.py:452, :507); the deadline path deliberately does not — workflow.py:645-646 'the staged fragments stay staged — recoverable by a re-run, which converges on the same rows because the unit ids are content-derived'. Abandoned bytes are now a stated recovery affordance with an orphan scan behind it (workflow.py:681-683), not a silent leak. [refuted-fix: The `terminate_chunks` half is real (workflow.py:656 and :723, activity at :1216-1237, ordering pinned by test_chunk_abandonment.py:107/122), but TWO of the finding's own harms survive untouched.  (a) THE STAGED FRAGMENTS ARE STILL ORPHANED — the finding's Fix said verbatim "make `purge_staged(uri, run_id)` run on every terminal path (not only inside `finalize_run`)". `grep -rn purge_staged services/ingest/src/ingest` returns exactly two call sites, both still inside `finalize_run`: runtime.py:452 and runtime.py:507. Neither early-terminal path calls `finalize` — the deadline branch goes :656 terminate -> :658 emit_terminal -> workflow.py:659 `return timed_out`, and the error boundary goes :723 terminate -> :724 emit_terminal -> workflow.py:725 `return failed`. `emit_terminal` (workflow.py:1096-1160) purges the queue and emits lineage and never touches staging. <details><summary><b>ratch-002</b> [**DISSOLVED**] — `ratch feature topics` reads the columns a fire-and-forget Ray Job has not written yet <i>(ratch, resilience, effort M)</i></summary>

**Sites:** `packages/ratch/src/ratch/core/jobs.py:133`, `packages/ratch/src/ratch/features/columns.py:327`, `packages/ratch/src/ratch/features/topic_tree.py:102`

**Why it matters.** With `RATCH_RAY_ENABLED=1` (the documented cluster mode — `jobs.py:19-24` and `runners.py:14-16` both name it), `run_runner` returns milliseconds after `submit_job`, so `build_topic_tree` scans `chunks.lance` before the Toponymy worker has committed anything. On a first run this raises `ValueError("no topic_l* columns … run 'ratch feature topics' first")` from inside the very command that IS `feature topics` — a self-contradicting error. On a re-run over a corpus that already has `topic_l*` columns it is worse than a crash: it silently rebuilds the tree from the PREVIOUS run's stale labels and reports success, because `overwrite_dataset(topics_path, table)` (topic_tree.py:117) unconditionally overwrites. The in-process branch (`ray_enabled=False`, `jobs.py:126-128` → `_run_in_process`) blocks correctly, so the bug only appears in the mode the docstrings call the intended one — exactly…

**Fix.** The submit-and-ack ruling is fine for a mover whose downstream is woken by the commit event, but `_run_topics` is a synchronous CLI step that consumes the output directly. Either (a) split the command so the tree build is a separate invocation that the job's completion event triggers (the shape `topic_tree` already exists for — `FEATURES["topic_tree"]`, columns.py:397), and have `_run_topics` return after dispatch with a message saying so; or (b) give `run_runner` an explicit `wait: bool` (defaulting to the current behaviour) and have `_run_topics` pass `wait=True`, restoring the terminal-status poll that `_TERMINAL_STATUSES`, `ray_job_timeout_seconds` and `ray_poll_interval_seconds` are…

**Verifier (CONFIRMED).** Code matches exactly. core/jobs.py:131-137: after `_submit_or_resubmit` the A13 SUBMIT-AND-ACK comment replaced the poll and `run_runner` returns immediately; the only blocking branch is `if not settings.ray_enabled: _run_in_process(job); return` at :126-128. features/columns.py:327-328 is `run_runner(RunnerJob(runner='topics', ...))` followed directly by `return _run_topic_tree(db_path, opts,…


**Re-verified 2026-08-24 — OPEN (severity now high).** core/jobs.py:126-137 is byte-identical to the audited text — the only blocking branch is still `if not settings.ray_enabled: _run_in_process(job); return` (:126-128), and the Ray branch still ends `logger.info("runner job %s dispatched; completion is its registered commit", submission_id)` (:137) under the SUBMIT-AND-ACK (A13) comment at :133. features/columns.py:327-328 still reads `run_runner(RunnerJob(runner="topics", entrypoint_args=("--db", db, *llm_args), token=db))` immediately followed by `return _run_topic_tree(db_path, opts, _progress)`. No `wait=` parameter was added to run_runner and no completion-triggered split exists; `_TERMINAL_STATUSES` (:50) is still declared and still only consumed by `_submit_or_resubmit`'s re-attach branch (:186).

**Current sites:** `/home/blackwell/Desktop/rask/packages/ratch/src/ratch/core/jobs.py:126`, `/home/blackwell/Desktop/rask/packages/ratch/src/ratch/core/jobs.py:137`, `/home/blackwell/Desktop/rask/packages/ratch/src/ratch/features/columns.py:327`


**RE-VERIFIED 2026-08-28 — CONFIRMED, high, effort M.** Correctly diagnosed, and the second outcome is why it is high: a RE-RUN over a corpus that already has `topic_l*` builds the tree from the PREVIOUS generation's labels, overwrites `topics.lance`, prints `done: N row(s) written`, and then the job commits generation-N columns that nothing ever rebuilds the tree from. The corpus is left permanently inconsistent WITH A SUCCESS MESSAGE ON THE RECORD, and never self-heals because ratch has no event lane to heal it. That is the crux of why A13's submit-and-ack was misapplied here: A13's second premise is 'the publication event off the job's commit wakes whatever comes next' — in ratch there is no such event and no such consumer. The fix is to give `run_runner` a return value (`RunnerOutcome.completed`) so the CLI can branch instead of assuming.

</details>
