### Detail

<details><summary><b>RV-01</b> — One shared `transform_version` column serves every stage on a table, so sibling stages invalidate each other — and on a blob table the loser's populated column is silently DROPPED and rebuilt <i>(ratch, high)</i></summary>

**Sites:** `/home/blackwell/Desktop/rask/packages/ratch/src/ratch/core/driver.py:50`, `/home/blackwell/Desktop/rask/packages/ratch/src/ratch/core/driver.py:330`, `/home/blackwell/Desktop/rask/packages/ratch/src/ratch/core/driver.py:353`, `/home/blackwell/Desktop/rask/packages/ratch/src/ratch/core/driver.py:301`, `/home/blackwell/Desktop/rask/packages/ratch/src/ratch/core/driver.py:307`, `/home/blackwell/Desktop/rask/packages/ratch/src/ratch/features/stages.py:23`

**Evidence.** B4 (commits f41bedea, 549c348c) introduced ONE table-wide column name: driver.py:50 `TRANSFORM_VERSION_COLUMN = "transform_version"` — not per output column. Every SCAN_COLUMN stage stamps ITS OWN identity into that single column for the rows it writes: driver.py:353-355 `stamp = pa.array([identity] * update.num_rows, pa.string())` / `update = update.append_column(pa.field(TRANSFORM_VERSION_COLUMN, pa.string()), stamp)` / `ds.merge_insert(key_columns).when_matched_update_all().execute(update)`, where `identity = stage.identity(actor_qualname=...)` (driver.py:325). It then RESUMES against the same shared column: driver.py:330 `predicate = resume_filter(name, identity=identity, has_version_column=has_version)`, and resume_filter (driver.py:76-95) returns `({output} IS NULL OR transform_version IS NULL OR transform_version != '{identity}')`.  The stage registry puts multiple stages on the same table: features/stages.py:23-38 `name="text_embedding", shape=SCAN_COLUMN, table="chunks"` and `name="summary", shape=SCAN_COLUMN, table="chunks"`; features/stages.py:43-68 `frame_embedding` (BLOB_COLUMN), `caption` (BLOB_COLUMN) and `caption_embedding` (SCAN_COLUMN) all on `table="chunk_frames"`.  Consequence A (waste): run `text_embedding` (every row stamped id_T), then `summary` (every row restamped id_S). A later re-run of `text_embedding` evaluates `transform_version != 'id_T'` → TRUE for every row, so `pending` counts the whole table and the already-correct `text_embedding` column is recomputed end-to-end on the GPU. The two stages perpetually invalidate one another; no run ever reaches "nothing to fill".  Consequence B (destructive): on `chunk_frames` the blob branch reads the same shared column — driver.py:301-303 `if TRANSFORM_VERSION_COLUMN in ds.schema.names:` / `seen = set(ds.to_table(columns=[TRANSFORM_VERSION_COLUMN]).column(TRANSFORM_VERSION_COLUMN).to_pylist())` / driver.py:307 `if blob_column_is_stale(seen, identity=blob_identity):` → driver.py:308-309 `logger.info("stage %s: %s was built by a superseded transform — rebuilding", ...)` / `ds.drop_columns([name])`. After `caption_embedding` (a SCAN stage) has stamped id_CE over the table, `blob_column_is_stale({id_CE}, identity=id_caption)` returns True (`known != {identity}`, driver.py:73), so the next `caption` or `frame_embedding` run DROPS its populated column and rebuilds it — a full re-caption of the corpus triggered by an unrelated sibling stage.  The new tests cannot see this: test_resume_filter.py and test_blob_transform_staleness.py drive `resume_filter`/`blob_column_is_stale` as pure functions with hand-supplied version sets, and no test runs two stages against one table.

**Fix.** Make the provenance column per output, not per table — e.g. `f"{name}__transform_version"` derived from `stage.output_columns`, or a single struct/map column keyed by output column name. Then `resume_filter` compares only the identity that wrote THAT column, and `blob_column_is_stale` reads only its own column's versions. Add a test that runs two stages on one table and asserts the first stage's second run reports `pending == 0`.

</details>

<details><summary><b>RV-02</b> — New ungated route `GET /api/assist/generation-schema` discloses any project's full label taxonomy to an unauthenticated caller <i>(annotator, med)</i></summary>

**Sites:** `services/annotator/src/annotator/api/v1/endpoints/assist.py:288`, `services/annotator/src/annotator/api/v1/endpoints/assist.py:289`, `services/annotator/src/annotator/projects/generation_schema.py:73`, `services/annotator/src/annotator/api/v1/endpoints/assist.py:34`

**Evidence.** Added by commit 6d29441e ('The ontology is a decode-time contract: JSON Schema for structured generation'), so it did not exist at 0ea0b7c2 (`git show 0ea0b7c2:.../assist.py | grep '@router\.'` lists only `/assist/producers` and `/assist/{doc_id}/{speech_id}/{chunk_id}`). Current assist.py:288-291: `@router.get("/assist/generation-schema")` / `async def generation_contract(task_id: str) -&gt; GenerationContract:` / docstring / `    return GenerationContract(output_schema=generation_schema(await _task_ontology(task_id)))` — the signature carries NO `CheckerDep` and NO `CurrentSubject`, unlike every route in projects.py/tasks.py/members.py/project_events.py. The router itself is ungated: assist.py:34 `router = APIRouter(prefix="/api", tags=["assist"])` (contrast jobs.py:47 `..., dependencies=[Depends(require_dapr_token)]` and tasks.py:84 `..., dependencies=[Depends(require_actor_plane)]`), and there is no app-level gate — main.py:166 `app = FastAPI(title="lance-media annotator", lifespan=lifespan)` with no `dependencies=`. The disclosure is real, not theoretical: `_task_ontology` (assist.py:359) reads the task actor by arbitrary id (`task = await _proxy(task_id).get()`, :371) and generation_schema.py:73 embeds the class names into the returned schema — `        "label": {"const": c.name},` — so the response is a verbatim dump of the project's class list, per-class tool restrictions and typed attributes. The neighbouring `GET /api/assist/producers?task_id=` (:210) leaks the same ontology indirectly, but that route predates the audit base.

**Fix.** Give the assist router the same gate the rest of the service uses: resolve the task's `project_id` from its actor document (as tasks.py:143 `_authorize` already does) and `await checker(user=subject, relation="can_view", obj=f"annotation_project:{project_id}")` before returning the schema. Cheapest correct form is a router-level `dependencies=[Depends(...)]` on assist.py:34 plus a per-task check inside `_task_ontology`'s callers, so `/assist/producers` and `POST /assist/...` inherit it too.

</details>

<details><summary><b>RV-03</b> — The new tenant-membership door repeats catalog-api-01: members.py raises service_kit's HTTPException taxonomy, bypassing the RFC 9457 problem handler <i>(catalog-api, med)</i></summary>

**Sites:** `services/catalog/src/catalog/api/v1/endpoints/members.py:37`, `services/catalog/src/catalog/api/v1/endpoints/members.py:110`, `services/catalog/src/catalog/api/v1/endpoints/members.py:194`

**Evidence.** members.py was ADDED after the audit base (`git diff --name-status 0ea0b7c2..HEAD` shows `A services/catalog/src/catalog/api/v1/endpoints/members.py`, landed with bb3d13b8 `feat(catalog,annotator): joining or leaving a project now tells the person`). It imports the fleet taxonomy at :37 `from service_kit.exceptions import ConflictError` and raises it on two client-visible paths: :110 `raise ConflictError("authorization is not configured on this deployment — tenant membership is unavailable")` (reached from PUT and DELETE /v1/projects/{id}/members via `_client_or_conflict`), and :193-196 `raise ConflictError(f"{user} holds the only remaining admin on this project — grant another admin first, or nobody will be able to administer or delete it")`. service_kit/exceptions.py:44 `class DomainError(HTTPException)` and :80 `class ConflictError(DomainError)`; the catalog installs only three handlers (ns_errors.py:123 LanceNamespaceError, :135 RequestValidationError, :148 Exception), so both answer a bare `{"detail": ...}` with content-type application/json — no spec `code`, not application/problem+json. The last-admin refusal is the one 409 a UI MUST branch on, and a generated Lance-Namespace client dispatching on `code` gets nothing.

**Fix.** Replace both with their lance_namespace equivalents — `ServiceUnavailableError` for the unconfigured-authz case (it is an availability fact, and it matches what access.py:113 already answers for the same condition) and `ConcurrentModificationError`/`InvalidInputError` for the last-admin refusal per the spec's 409 mapping — and drop the service_kit.exceptions import. Then close the class of defect rather than the instance: add the contract test catalog-api-01's fix already asks for (assert `content-type == application/problem+json` and a present `code`) and extend it over every 4xx/5xx-producing route, so the next module cannot import the wrong taxonomy silently. `grep -rn 'service_kit.exceptions' services/catalog/src/catalog/api/` returns exactly two modules today — stores.py and members.py — which makes this a two-file fix and a one-test gate.


**FIXED (2026-08-28)** with `catalog-api-01` — same defect, one commit; see that finding's closure.

</details>

<details><summary><b>RV-04</b> — The lineage emitter's project fallback is keyed on the PARENT namespace id, not the top-level segment — every nested table's create event reaches zero watchers <i>(catalog-core, med)</i></summary>

**Sites:** `services/catalog/src/catalog/core/lineage_emit.py:587`, `services/catalog/src/catalog/api/v1/endpoints/data.py:293`, `services/catalog/src/catalog/api/v1/endpoints/data.py:168`

**Evidence.** The resolver is keyed on the TOP-LEVEL namespace segment. main.py:154-162 `async def _resolve_project(top_ns: str)` → :159 `warehouses.project_for_namespace(settings.registry_root, settings.storage_options(), top_ns)`, which (warehouses.py:289 → :265-269 `binding_for_namespace`) reads `_warehouses/bindings/{top_ns}.json` — and bindings are only ever written keyed on a top-level namespace (warehouses.py:247-248 `payload = {"top_ns": top_ns, ...}` / `key = f"{_BINDINGS_PREFIX}/{top_ns}.json"`).  `emit_write_event` gets this right — lineage_emit.py:736 `project = await emitter.project_for(segments[0] if segments else "")`, with the comment at :731 'THE TENANT, resolved from the table's TOP namespace segment'.  The fallback inside the emitter does NOT. lineage_emit.py:587: `resolved_project = project or await self.project_for(namespace)` — where `namespace` is the PARENT namespace id, all segments but the last (service_kit/governed/fga.py:197-201 `return canonical_object_id(segments[:-1], delimiter=delimiter)`).  The one caller that relies on that fallback is the table-create door: `grep -rn 'emit_create(\|\.emit_write(' services/catalog/src/catalog --include=*.py | grep -v core/lineage_emit.py` returns exactly one hit, api/v1/endpoints/data.py:293 `await emitter.emit_create(table_id=table_id, namespace=namespace, ...)` — and its kwargs (:294-303) include no `project`. `namespace` there is data.py:168 `namespace = fga.parent_namespace_id(segments, delimiter=settings.delimiter) or ""`.  So for a table at `acme-bronze$sub$pages`, the fallback calls `project_for("acme-bronze$sub")`, which looks up `_warehouses/bindings/acme-bronze$sub.json` — never written — returns None, and build_write_event's guard at :232 `if project and is_safe_project(project):` omits the `lance.project` facet. Nested namespaces are a supported first-class shape (core/identifiers.py:56 `MAX_NAMESPACE_DEPTH = 8`, with two walkers that recurse), so this is not a theoretical path. Per the emitter's own comment at :218-221, notifications' fan-out 'skips the watcher loop ENTIRELY when it is absent' — the create reaches its author and no watchers, silently, with a SUCCESS ack.

**Fix.** Give `_BaseLineageEmitter.emit_write` the top-level segment rather than the parent-namespace id. Either derive it in the emitter (`namespace.split(self._delimiter)[0]`) or — cleaner, and matching how emit_write_event already does it — have data.py resolve and pass `project=` explicitly at the emit_create call site, the way endpoints/columns.py reaches the correct value via emit_write_event. Pin it with a test asserting `lance.project == "acme"` for a create of `acme-bronze$sub$pages`, so the two resolution keys cannot drift again.

</details>

<details><summary><b>RV-05</b> — medallion's produce door verifies OIDC bearers inline on the event loop — the fix that landed in ingest was never applied to its twin <i>(cross-service, med)</i></summary>

**Sites:** `services/medallion/src/medallion/api/produce_auth.py:124`, `services/medallion/src/medallion/api/produce_auth.py:169`, `services/ingest/src/ingest/auth.py:176`

**Evidence.** `services/medallion/src/medallion/api/produce_auth.py:58 async def authorize_produce(...)` calls, at line 124, `token = verifier.verify(raw)` — and `authenticate_subject` (`async def`, line 133) does the same at line 169 (`return verifier.verify(raw).sub`). `OIDCVerifier.verify` is synchronous and performs blocking network I/O: packages/service-kit/src/service_kit/governed/oidc.py:186 `with httpx.Client(timeout=15.0) as client:` for discovery/JWKS. The sibling door already fixed this — services/ingest/src/ingest/auth.py:176 `token = await asyncio.to_thread(verifier.verify, raw)` with the comment 'Off the loop: verify() does synchronous OIDC discovery + JWKS fetches (up to 15s) on a cold cache or key rotation — inline it stalled every in-flight request in the pod, probes included (open_python-audit ING-02).' Every other verify call site in the estate is inside a plain `def` and is therefore threadpooled (catalog/api/security.py:41, lineage/api/security.py:138, annotator/api/security.py:43). medallion's two are the only `async def` ones.

**Fix.** Wrap both calls as `await asyncio.to_thread(verifier.verify, raw)`, matching ingest/auth.py:176, and add the assertion to the DUP-03 extraction when it happens so the shared door can only have one answer.

</details>

<details><summary><b>RV-06</b> — ingest's ING-01 blank-service-token fail-closed guard was not mirrored into medallion's produce door <i>(cross-service, med)</i></summary>

**Sites:** `services/medallion/src/medallion/api/produce_auth.py:98`, `services/ingest/src/ingest/auth.py:139`

**Evidence.** services/ingest/src/ingest/auth.py:139 now reads `if not expected and not (settings.oidc_enabled or settings.fga_enabled): return None`, with a comment naming the bug it closes: 'an estate with `LANCE_OIDC_ENABLED=true`, a live FGA client and a blank or unset `APP_API_TOKEN` accepted every ingest from anyone who could reach the port, while every surface reported authorization as ON. That is open_python-audit's ING-01'. The copy of that same door in medallion still has the pre-fix shape — services/medallion/src/medallion/api/produce_auth.py:98-101: `expected = os.environ.get("APP_API_TOKEN")` then `if not expected: return None`, unconditionally. `/produce` is the cascade head (it seeds bronze, fabricates OpenLineage provenance and fires bronze→silver→gold) and `/train` spends GPU, both reached through `authorize_produce`.

**Fix.** Apply the same condition in produce_auth.py — open only when no service token AND neither `settings.oidc_enabled` nor `settings.fga_enabled` is set. This is the concrete cost of DUP-03 remaining open.

</details>

<details><summary><b>RV-07</b> — The inline lane emits no node metric — `flows.nodes`, the service's stated only failure signal, is blind for every non-durable run <i>(flows-fleet, med)</i></summary>

**Sites:** `services/flows/src/flows/activities.py:49`, `services/flows/src/flows/executor.py:273-299`, `services/flows/src/flows/metrics.py:49-56`, `services/flows/src/flows/routes.py:212-220`

**Evidence.** `grep -rn 'record_node' services/flows/src` returns exactly two hits: the definition at metrics.py:49 and ONE call site, activities.py:49 `record_node(result.state.status)` — inside the Dapr activity, i.e. the durable lane only. The inline lane runs routes.py:212-220 `record_run("inline")` / `state = await executor.execute(...)` -&gt; executor.py:347 `await asyncio.gather(*(run_node(job, client=client) ...))` -&gt; executor.py:273-299 `executor.run_node`, which constructs the `NodeResult` and returns it without ever touching `flows.metrics`. The gap is exactly the one the metrics module's own docstring says it exists to close: metrics.py:10-12 — "And the INLINE lane has no workflow at all, so no Dapr family observes it even in principle." The inline lane is the ONLY lane whenever `DAPR_GRPC_PORT` is unset (lifespan.py:100/:128) and is also where every degraded run lands (routes.py:190-201, 209-220), so a node failure in a sidecar-less deployment or after a durable-lane degrade is counted by nothing at all.

**Fix.** Call `record_node(result.state.status)` inside `executor.run_node` (both return paths) rather than in `activities.py`, and drop the activity's call — that is the single function both lanes share, which is the same argument the module already makes for putting the payload caps on `models.NodeResult` instead of at its three construction sites. Add a test asserting the counter moves for an inline run.

</details>

<details><summary><b>RV-08</b> — The cron tick bypasses the accept-time source refusal the HTTP door was just given — a refused source still gets a run record and a provisioned table <i>(ingest-domain, med)</i></summary>

**Sites:** `services/ingest/src/ingest/cron.py:99`, `services/ingest/src/ingest/api.py:384`, `services/ingest/src/ingest/api.py:388`

**Evidence.** commit 634a8099 (2026-08-23) added `_refuse_unusable_source` to close "a refused source got a 202 and a table it was refused permission to make", wiring it at api.py:383-385: ```     originator = await authorize_ingest(request, settings, body.project, ...)     await _refuse_unusable_source(body)     return await dispatch_run(body, response, store=store, starter=starter, ...) ``` The guard sits OUTSIDE `dispatch_run`, and `grep -rn '_refuse_unusable_source' services/ingest/` shows exactly one production call site (api.py:384). The cron tick, added the same day by e629e2cc, calls `dispatch_run` directly — cron.py:99-109 `accepted = await dispatch_run(IngestRequest.model_validate(spec), Response(), store=state.run_store, starter=state.workflow_starter, idempotency_key=f"cron-{uuid.uuid4().hex}", originator=None)` — with no `_refuse_unusable_source` anywhere in the module. cron.py:70-74 nevertheless claims the opposite: "One poll — dispatched through the SAME path the HTTP door uses… a second way to start a run would have to re-derive the deterministic run id, the idempotency dedupe and the claim/release". Consequence, in 634a8099's own words: `ensure_dataset` (workflow.py) provisions the target table BEFORE enumerate reaches the guards, so a cron-configured source refused for a SECURITY reason (outside RASK_INGEST_LANCE_ROOT, or under LANCE_REST_ROOT — adapters.py `confine_to_lance_root`) creates the table anyway, once per tick, forever, with the refusal buried in `errors.run`.

**Fix.** Move `_refuse_unusable_source(body)` to the head of `dispatch_run` so both entry points inherit it, and delete the now-redundant call at api.py:384. That also makes cron.py's "SAME path" docstring true. Add a cron-path test alongside services/ingest/tests/test_accept_door_refuses_source.py.

</details>

<details><summary><b>RV-09</b> — The chunk-pointer change made every chunk download and JSON-parse the run's ENTIRE unit manifest, synchronously, on the event loop <i>(ingest-flow, med)</i></summary>

**Sites:** `services/ingest/src/ingest/staging.py:153`, `services/ingest/src/ingest/staging.py:165`, `services/ingest/src/ingest/staging.py:173`, `services/ingest/src/ingest/runtime.py:250`

**Evidence.** Commit 8600cd67 ('chunk descriptors become POINTERS — the unit ceiling is gone, not just named') replaced inline chunk keys with an (offset, count) window into one per-run manifest. The read is whole-file: staging.py:161-175 `uri = unit_manifest_uri(dataset_uri, run_id)` ... `raw = _client().get_object(Bucket=bucket, Key=key)["Body"].read().decode()` ... `units = json.loads(raw).get("units") or []` ... `window = units[offset : offset + count]` — the full object is fetched and the full JSON array is parsed in order to slice out one chunk's window. There is no cache: `read_unit_slice` is a module-level function with no memoisation, and `_client()` is likewise uncached (staging.py:404). It is called once PER CHUNK, from inside an async activity body: runtime.py:242-250 `if chunk.keys: ... else:` / `from ingest.staging import read_unit_slice` / `pairs = read_unit_slice(chunk.dataset_uri, chunk.run_id, chunk.offset, chunk.count)`, inside `async def publish_chunk_units(chunk: ChunkSpec)` (runtime.py:223). Two defects in one call: (1) at the module's own stated scale — a 1M-unit run at CHUNK_SIZE=1000 — this is 1000 full downloads and 1000 full parses of a manifest holding 1,000,000 [key, token] pairs, i.e. O(chunks x units) object-store bytes and CPU where the pointer design intended O(units) total; (2) it is a blocking boto3 GET plus a blocking `json.loads` of a large document executed directly in an `async def` with no `run_in_threadpool`/`asyncio.to_thread`, stalling the event loop of the pod running that activity. The original audit could not have seen this: `read_unit_slice` does not exist at 0ea0b7c2 (`git show 0ea0b7c2:services/ingest/src/ingest/staging.py | grep read_unit_slice` returns nothing).

**Fix.** Read the manifest once per activity process and slice from memory — an `lru_cache`d loader keyed on (dataset_uri, run_id) is enough given a chunk's activity is short-lived, or better, write the manifest as one object PER CHUNK at enumerate time so a pointer read is O(chunk) by construction (staging roots are already per-run; adding the chunk segment is the same move finding 03's fix needs). Whichever shape, wrap the object-store read and the parse in `asyncio.to_thread` so `publish_chunk_units` stops blocking its loop, and reuse a cached `_client()` rather than constructing one per call (ingest-flow-13).

</details>

<details><summary><b>RV-10</b> — A dataset whose COMPACTION alone is refused (feature flag 16) is never cadence-stamped, so its interval policy is ignored forever and it is fully re-swept every tick <i>(maintenance, med)</i></summary>

**Sites:** `services/maintenance/src/maintenance/services/sweep.py:356`, `services/maintenance/src/maintenance/services/optimize.py:250`, `services/maintenance/src/maintenance/services/sweep.py:360`

**Evidence.** Two post-audit commits interact. 18615b13 (T4) added `refused is None` to the cadence-stamp guard — sweep.py:356: `            if policy is not None and policy.get("compact_interval_hours") and result.error is None and result.refused is None:` with the justification at sweep.py:360-362: `` `refused is None` is load-bearing and was missing. A refusal carries `error=None` by construction (optimize.py returns `DatasetResult(uri=…, refused=…)` with no error), so a dataset the sweep can never maintain was stamped as freshly maintained``. The LATER commit 5657a164 (T2) falsified that premise. `compact_one` now sets `refused` on a result that is NOT an early return and that goes on to do real work — optimize.py:246-251: ```         if compact_refusal is not None:             # Root-scoped work still runs below; only the rewrite is skipped. Recorded on the result so             # the reason is visible without inferring it from a zero, and logged once per dataset.             log.info("maintenance_compaction_skipped_unsupported", extra={"uri": uri, "reason": compact_refusal})             result.refused = compact_refusal             metrics: Any = None ``` Control then falls through to `ds.optimize.optimize_indices()` (optimize.py:288) and `ds.cleanup_old_versions(...)` (optimize.py:384), which set `indices_optimized` / `old_versions_removed` / `bytes_removed`. Commit order confirms the regression direction: `git log --oneline --reverse 0ea0b7c2..HEAD -- .../sweep.py .../optimize.py` lists 18615b13 before 5657a164. Consequence: for the 17 flag-16 datasets 5657a164's own message says this estate carries, `result.refused` is always truthy, so `maintenance_policies.write_state` (sweep.py:369) never runs, `_policy_skip_reason` never finds a stamp (sweep.py:82-89), and `compact_interval_hours` — the operator's pacing lever — is silently inert. Every 120s tick reopens the dataset, re-runs `optimize_indices` and `cleanup_old_versions` on it, and `record_refused` (sweep.py:394) plus `summarize`'s `"refused"` line (sweep.py:497) count it as a dataset that did nothing.

**Fix.** Distinguish a TOTAL refusal (early return, no work) from a PARTIAL one (compaction skipped, root-scoped work done). Either give `DatasetResult` a separate field for the partial case, or make the stamp guard test for work actually performed rather than for `refused is None` — e.g. stamp when `result.error is None and (result.refused is None or _did_material_work(result) or result.indices_optimized)`. The sweep.py:360-365 comment must be corrected in the same change: its "by construction" claim is no longer true.

</details>

<details><summary><b>RV-11</b> — GET /promotions/{instance_id} skips its FGA check entirely for an anonymous caller, while POST .../decision refuses one <i>(medallion, med)</i></summary>

**Sites:** `services/medallion/src/medallion/api/promotions.py:262`, `services/medallion/src/medallion/api/promotions.py:251`, `services/medallion/src/medallion/api/produce_auth.py:157`

**Evidence.** `show` gates conditionally: promotions.py:262-264 `gate = _fga_gate(request)` / `if gate is not None and subject:` / `await gate(subject=subject, obj=promotion_object(spec))` — so a falsy `subject` skips the `can_promote` check and the route returns the full `PromotionUnderReview` (project, from_dataset, to_dataset, the failed-assertion `reasons`, approval_hours) at :265-272. `subject` is falsy for any request with no Authorization header, because produce_auth.py:156-158 is `verifier ... = getattr(request.app.state, "oidc", None)` / `if not authorization:` / `    return None` — it returns None rather than raising. The sibling write route does the opposite: decide_promotion refuses it at promotions.py:189-191 `if not subject:` ... `raise PermissionDeniedError("a promotion decision must name the person who made it; sign in and retry")`. The router carries no compensating dependency — promotions.py:38 `router = APIRouter(tags=["promotions"])` — and producer.py:175 mounts it bare: `app.include_router(promotions_router)`.

**Fix.** Refuse an unauthenticated caller on `show` the same way `decide_promotion` does — `if not subject: raise PermissionDeniedError(...)` before the `_live_spec` read — and make the gate unconditional once FGA is on (`if gate is not None: await gate(...)`), so a missing subject cannot be the thing that satisfies the condition. The promotion-review routes were added after the audit base (commits a71c12a5 / 059e4fae), so no earlier pass could have seen this.

</details>

<details><summary><b>RV-12</b> — Blocking DaprWorkflowClient gRPC calls run on the event loop inside async handlers, while the sibling module threadpools the identical calls <i>(medallion, med)</i></summary>

**Sites:** `services/medallion/src/medallion/services/transform.py:545`, `services/medallion/src/medallion/services/transform.py:130`, `services/medallion/src/medallion/services/train.py:301`, `services/medallion/src/medallion/services/train.py:330`

**Evidence.** `_dispatch_stage_workflow` is a plain `def` (transform.py:83) that builds and calls the sync client — :130 `client = wf.DaprWorkflowClient()`, :132 `client.schedule_new_workflow(workflow=stage_run, input=spec.model_dump(), instance_id=instance_id)`, :158 `return client.get_workflow_state(instance_id) is not None` — and it is invoked WITHOUT `run_in_threadpool` from the async handler: transform.py:545 `instance_id = _dispatch_stage_workflow(` inside `async def handle_stage` (:235). Same shape in the train lane: train.py:301 `schedule_train_watch(settings, token=token, ...)` called bare from `async def handle_train_trigger` (:183), whose body is :330-331 `client = wf.DaprWorkflowClient()` / `client.schedule_new_workflow(workflow=train_run, ...)`. The estate's own convention is the opposite and lives one directory over: promotions.py:145 `await run_in_threadpool(lambda: wf_client.schedule_new_workflow(...))`, :203 `await run_in_threadpool(lambda: wf_client.raise_workflow_event(...))`, :262 `spec = await run_in_threadpool(_live_spec, wf_client, instance_id)`.

**Fix.** Wrap both dispatch seams in `run_in_threadpool` at the call site (transform.py:545, train.py:301), matching promotions.py. A slow or unreachable sidecar currently stalls the whole mover/producer event loop — every concurrent Dapr delivery and every probe — rather than one worker.

</details>

<details><summary><b>RV-13</b> — The #140 job_logs rewrite duplicated PS-17's status-swallow onto the 5-second RUNNING-job poll path <i>(packages-small, med)</i></summary>

**Sites:** `packages/ray-kit/src/ray_kit/dashboard.py:621`, `packages/ray-kit/src/ray_kit/dashboard.py:623`

**Evidence.** At the audit base, job_logs went through the SDK and had no HTTP status branch:   $ git show 0ea0b7c2:packages/ray-kit/src/ray_kit/dashboard.py | sed -n '510,521p'   async def job_logs(client: JobSubmissionClient | None, submission_id: str, tail: int = 2000) -&gt; RayJobLogsPayload:       """Driver logs for one submitted job (last `tail` lines), via the Jobs SDK."""       ...           text = await to_thread.run_sync(client.get_job_logs, submission_id) Commit 380e8818 replaced it with a raw bounded GET and copied logs()'s swallow:   packages/ray-kit/src/ray_kit/dashboard.py:621-623       if resp.status_code &gt;= HTTPStatus.BAD_REQUEST:           # Ray's log endpoint 500s on empty files — same note logs() uses.           return RayJobLogsPayload(ok=True, submission_id=submission_id, logs="(empty or unavailable)") So a 401/403 from a token-authed dashboard, or a 404 for a driver log on a node that has rotated away, now renders as an EMPTY driver log with ok=True — on the path the compute zone polls every 5 s while a job runs. The audit could not have seen this: the branch did not exist at 0ea0b7c2.

**Fix.** Distinguish the one status Ray actually uses for an empty file (500 with an empty body) from 4xx: return ok=False with the status code for &gt;=400 other than that case, and let the UI say 'log unavailable (HTTP 401)' rather than showing an empty pane. Apply the same split to logs() (PS-17) so the two stay one rule, not two copies.

</details>

<details><summary><b>RV-14</b> — services/gateway builds its own FastAPI app and configures NO logging — the one service the estate-wide logging fix missed, and the drift guard cannot see it <i>(service-kit-core, med)</i></summary>

**Sites:** `services/gateway/src/gateway/__init__.py:280`, `services/gateway/src/gateway/__init__.py:36`, `packages/service-kit/src/service_kit/obs.py:25`, `tests/unit/test_invariants.py:3850`

**Evidence.** `grep -rn 'setup_logging|configure_app_logging|basicConfig' services/gateway/src/` returns NOTHING. gateway/__init__.py:280 `app = FastAPI(title="gateway", version="0.1.0", lifespan=lifespan)` — it does not go through `make_service_app`, so `service_kit.setup_logging()` (which attaches the `rask-stdout` handler to root and applies RASK_LOG_LEVEL) never runs; gateway/__init__.py:285 calls only `setup_otel(app, service_name="gateway")`. gateway/__init__.py:36 `log = logging.getLogger("gateway")`, and `"gateway"` is absent from `obs._APP_LOGGERS` (obs.py:25-44 lists catalog, lineage, medallion, maintenance, common, service_kit, viewer, search, annotator, ratch). Root therefore stays at WARNING with no handler, so gateway/__init__.py:273 `log.info(f"route {prefix} -&gt; {app_id} ({_target_base(app_id, fallback)}{upstream_prefix})")` — the only record of which upstream each /api prefix resolves to — is dropped, and gateway/__init__.py:259 `log.warning(f"openapi fetch failed for {app_id}: {exc}")` propagates to a handler-less root and is discarded too. Contrast the eight services fixed by cf78ca0f, each of which calls it explicitly: catalog/main.py:242, lineage/main.py:127, medallion/producer.py:150, etc.

**Fix.** Call `service_kit.setup_logging()` at gateway module import (next to the existing `setup_otel` call), and widen the invariant guard: tests/unit/test_invariants.py:3850 derives `callers` only from files that already contain `configure_app_logging(`, so a service that configures no logging at all is structurally invisible to it. Assert instead that every `services/*` package either calls `setup_logging()`/`make_service_app` or calls `configure_app_logging()`.

</details>

<details><summary><b>RV-15</b> — records.py builds its own boto3 S3 client, bypassing storage.s3_client — and drops the TLS/CA handling on the estate's id-minting doors <i>(service-kit-governed, med)</i></summary>

**Sites:** `packages/service-kit/src/service_kit/lakehouse/records.py:58`, `packages/service-kit/src/service_kit/lakehouse/records.py:59`, `packages/service-kit/src/service_kit/lakehouse/records.py:61`, `services/catalog/src/catalog/services/projects.py:26`, `services/catalog/src/catalog/services/warehouses.py:27`

**Evidence.** records.py (NEW since 0ea0b7c2 — it carries the conditional put-if-not-exists seam) hand-rolls the client at :58-70: 'def _s3_client(storage_options): import boto3; from botocore.config import Config; return boto3.client("s3", endpoint_url=storage_options.get("endpoint"), aws_access_key_id=..., aws_secret_access_key=..., region_name=... or "us-east-1", config=Config(s3={"addressing_style": "path"}))'. Compare the canonical wrapper, packages/storage/src/storage/client.py:73-103, which additionally sets signature_version="s3v4", connect_timeout=10, read_timeout=60, retries={"max_attempts": 3, "mode": "adaptive"}, the checksum flags, unregisters needs-retry.s3, and — the load-bearing part — resolves TLS: 'if ca := _env_first(_CA_BUNDLE_ENVS): kwargs["verify"] = ca; elif skip_verify: kwargs["verify"] = False'. records.py sets no verify at all. grep -rn 'import boto3' over packages/ and services/ (excluding tests) returns 5 hits; records.py:59 is the only one in service-kit. The consumers are the id-minting doors: services/catalog/src/catalog/services/projects.py:26 and services/catalog/src/catalog/services/warehouses.py:27 both 'from service_kit.lakehouse.records import create_json, mutate_json'.

**Fix.** Call storage.s3_client(endpoint=..., access_key=..., secret_key=...) — it already takes per-client credential overrides for exactly this multi-backend case — and pass IfNoneMatch/IfMatch through it, rather than constructing a second, differently-configured client. If a distinct config is genuinely required, add the option to storage.s3_client so one wrapper stays the single S3 boundary.

</details>

<details><summary><b>RV-16</b> — The seam's own no-workload-vocabulary gate is green while the seam names two workloads — its AST visitor cannot see settings fields, and 'diarization' does not contain 'diarize' <i>(viewer-search, med)</i></summary>

**Sites:** `services/viewer/tests/test_no_workload_vocabulary_at_the_seam.py:31`, `services/viewer/tests/test_no_workload_vocabulary_at_the_seam.py:47`, `services/viewer/src/viewer/core/config.py:38`, `services/viewer/src/viewer/api/v1/endpoints/diarization.py:28`, `services/viewer/src/viewer/services/serve_encoder.py:59`

**Evidence.** The gate (added post-audit in c22c00d2) declares FORBIDDEN = ('transcript','transcribe','speech','htr','ocr','asr','diarize','voiceprint','handwriting') at test_no_workload_vocabulary_at_the_seam.py:31-41 and asserts over `_public_names` (:47-58), which collects ONLY FunctionDef/AsyncFunctionDef names, their positional arg names, string constants that `startswith('/')`, and constants under a `tags=`/`prefix=` keyword. Two live violations slip through both filters: (1) SUBSTRING MISS. `diarize` is forbidden but `diarization` does not contain it. Verified: python3 -c "print([w for w in FORBIDDEN if w in '/api/diarization/{doc_id}'])" -&gt; []. So services/viewer/src/viewer/api/v1/endpoints/diarization.py:28 `router = APIRouter(prefix="/api/diarization", tags=["diarization"])`, the handler `get_diarization` (:46), the module stem, and services/viewer/src/viewer/schemas/diarization.py all pass. (2) NODE-TYPE MISS. services/viewer/src/viewer/core/config.py:38 `voiceprint_serve_url: str = Field(default="", alias="VIEWER_VOICEPRINT_SERVE_URL")` DOES contain a forbidden word (python3 check returns ['voiceprint']) but is an AnnAssign, which `_public_names` never walks. Same for the caller-visible 503 bodies in services/viewer/src/viewer/services/serve_encoder.py:59 `f"voiceprint runner refused the embed ({response.status_code}): …"` and :64 `f"voiceprint runner unreachable at {self._url}"`, and voice.py:66-68 "the voiceprint runner is not configured (VIEWER_VOICEPRINT_SERVE_URL)". Measured green: `uv run pytest services/viewer/tests/test_no_workload_vocabulary_at_the_seam.py -q` -&gt; 70 passed in 0.23s. The irony is that serve_encoder.py's own docstring (:17-19) claims "NOTHING HERE IS MODALITY-SPECIFIC, and the name says so" while its error strings name a runner.

**Fix.** Match on word STEMS rather than whole words ('diariz', 'transcri'), and extend `_public_names` to walk `ast.AnnAssign` targets plus `Field(alias=...)` literals so config surface and public error bodies are in scope. Then either rename the diarization route family to a descriptor-resolved concept (as transcripts.py was) or record it as an explicit, justified allowlist entry — silence from a gate that cannot see the violation is worse than no gate.

</details>

<details><summary><b>RV-17</b> — An extraction that already exists in service-kit was bypassed twice by services added since the audit <i>(cross-service, low)</i></summary>

**Sites:** `services/notifications/src/notifications/health.py:14`, `services/flows/src/flows/lifespan.py:68`

**Evidence.** Two new copies of already-flagged duplications entered the tree after the base commit. (1) services/notifications/src/notifications/health.py:14-20 is a fifth byte-equivalent liveness router (`router = APIRouter(tags=["health"])` / `@router.get("/health")` / `async def health() -&gt; Liveness: return Liveness()`), even though the same service mounts `make_probes_router` at notifications/__init__.py:34 — so it carries both conventions at once. (2) services/flows/src/flows/lifespan.py:68-88 is a ninth copy of the DUP-01 governed-auth bootstrap (`app.state.oidc = OIDCVerifier(...)` … `store_id, model_id = await fga.provision(...)` … `app.state.fga = fga.make_client(...)`) with the swallow-and-log failure posture.

**Fix.** Nothing new to design — these are DUP-06 and DUP-01. Worth recording separately because they show the duplication is still actively reproducing, so the extraction is now cheaper than continuing to add copies.

</details>

<details><summary><b>RV-18</b> — ingest hosts a Dapr sidecar-delivered route while declaring `daprIngest: false` and never calling `assert_app_token_configured` <i>(ingest-domain, low)</i></summary>

**Sites:** `services/ingest/src/ingest/cron.py:69`, `services/ingest/src/ingest/cron.py:130`, `services/ingest/src/ingest/__init__.py:63`, `chart/values.yaml:139`

**Evidence.** cron.py:130 `router.add_api_route(f"/{binding_name}", _on_tick, methods=["POST"], tags=["ingest-cron"])`, mounted at __init__.py:63 `if mount_incremental_cron(app, os.environ.get("RASK_INGEST_CRON_BINDING_NAME")):`. Its only guard is cron.py:69 `async def _on_tick(request: Request, _: Annotated[None, Depends(require_dapr_token)])`. packages/service-kit/src/service_kit/governed/dapr_auth.py:80-82 states the precondition that guard relies on: "The token check is a no-op when ``APP_API_TOKEN`` is unset — the open dev default; ``assert_app_token_configured`` makes that a startup error once Dapr ingest is actually enabled, so the no-op can only apply in dev." `grep -rn 'assert_app_token_configured' services/ingest/` returns nothing. chart/values.yaml:139 sets `daprIngest: false` for ingest, so chart/templates/fleet.yaml:157-160 renders APP_API_TOKEN only through the `lanceWriter and (not governedAuth or auth.enabled)` arm; with `--set auth.enabled=false` (chart/values.yaml:706 defaults it true) plus `services.ingest.cronBindingName` set, chart/templates/ingest-cron.yaml renders the input binding and the tick route is live with no token to compare against.

**Fix.** Call `assert_app_token_configured(dapr_enabled=bool(os.environ.get("RASK_INGEST_CRON_BINDING_NAME")))` in `create_app()` before `mount_incremental_cron`, and flip `services.ingest.daprIngest` to true whenever `cronBindingName` is non-empty so the token renders with the route. Add the startup-guard test the ING-01 fix omitted.

</details>

<details><summary><b>RV-19</b> — The new run-mutation authz check fails OPEN when app.state.repository is absent, 30 lines from a sibling check that 503s on the same condition <i>(lineage, low)</i></summary>

**Sites:** `services/lineage/src/lineage/api/fga_deps.py:184`, `services/lineage/src/lineage/api/fga_deps.py:185`, `services/lineage/src/lineage/api/fga_deps.py:253`

**Evidence.** Added by commit 92bb8e3a ("amending an existing run is authorized by the data that run wrote"), after the audit base. fga_deps.py:184-185:      repository = getattr(request.app.state, "repository", None)     if repository is not None and event.run.run_id:         prior = await repository.run_output_names(event.run.run_id)  An absent `app.state.repository` silently SKIPS the whole run-mutation check — the request proceeds to the ordinary output check, which its own comment says is vacuous for an event naming no dataset ("an event naming NO dataset used to be authorized having checked nothing and still rewrote the run: set `operation` to `drop_table` and the reconcile sweep skips that dataset forever, or set FAIL and serve an invented author to every viewer"). The same file resolves the analogous missing dependency the opposite way, 68 lines below at :253-255:      client = getattr(self._request.app.state, "fga", None)     if client is None:         raise ServiceUnavailableError("authorization service is not available")  So one missing piece of app.state is a fail-closed 503 and the other is a silent pass. In the deployed service `app.state.repository` is always set (main.py:74) — the exposure is any app assembled without the lifespan, which is precisely how the ingest routes are exercised in tests, meaning a regression in this control can go green.

**Fix.** Mirror the FGA branch: raise `ServiceUnavailableError("lineage graph is not available")` when `app.state.repository` is None, rather than treating absence as 'no run to protect'. The load-bearing empty answer is `run_output_names()` returning `[]`, which the repository already gives for a run that does not exist — the getattr guard adds a second, unintended way to get the same permissive outcome.

</details>

<details><summary><b>RV-20</b> — ServeEncoder builds a fresh httpx.Client per embed call — the replacement for the deleted in-process encoder reintroduces VS-12's per-call-client pattern <i>(viewer-search, low)</i></summary>

**Sites:** `services/viewer/src/viewer/services/serve_encoder.py:55`, `services/viewer/src/viewer/api/v1/endpoints/voice.py:73`

**Evidence.** serve_encoder.py:52-65 `def embed_batch(self, waveforms: list[np.ndarray]) -&gt; np.ndarray:` / `rows: list[np.ndarray] = []` / `try:` / `with httpx.Client(timeout=self._timeout) as client:` / `for waveform in waveforms: … client.post(self._url, content=body, …)`. A new client, connection pool and TLS handshake per call. Compounding it, `voice_encoder` (voice.py:49-73) constructs a NEW `ServeEncoder(url)` on every request rather than caching it on `AppState` the way `clients.py`'s `ensure_embedder`/`ensure_reranker` do for the search service (`state.embedder`/`state.reranker`). Introduced by commit cda709f0, so the original audit could not have seen it.

**Fix.** Give ServeEncoder a long-lived pooled `httpx.Client` built once (closed in the lifespan alongside `state.http`/`state.embedder`/`state.reranker`, main.py:81), and cache the encoder on AppState the way `search.services.clients` already does.

</details>

### From the `writing-python` reference sweep

All 10 `writing-python` references were read in full on 2026-08-24 and applied to the estate. Two
findings survived; everything else the references check was already clean (no bare `except:`, no mutable
default arguments, no `lru_cache` on instance methods, only 2 `assert`s — both type-narrowing with a
`# narrowed:` comment, which is the one use the reference sanctions).

<details><summary><b>WP-01</b> — 19 legacy `TypeVar(` / `ParamSpec(` sites; PEP 695 is the form <i>(cross-service, low)</i></summary>

**Sites:** `services/flows/src/flows/catalog.py` (12 `ParamSpec`), `services/flows/src/flows/models.py` (1),
`services/search/src/search/services/encoders/base.py` (3 `TypeVar`), `packages/ratch/src/ratch/clients/base.py` (3 `TypeVar`)

**Why it matters.** `writing-python` → `references/type-safety.md` is explicit: PEP 695 type-parameter
syntax (`class Foo[T]`, `def deco[**P, R](...)`) is the form in this project, and "`TypeVar` from `typing`
still works but is legacy — don't reach for it in new code". This is the *other half* of the rule this
audit already checks: the scorecard's "**Zero** legacy `Optional[…]`/`Union[…]`" claim is true and was
measured, but the same reference makes the same demand of type parameters and that half was never swept.
Low severity — it is modernisation, not a defect — but it is filed so the "we are clean on legacy typing"
claim stops being half-true.

**Fix.** Convert the four modules to PEP 695. `flows/catalog.py` is the bulk (12 sites, a decorator
registry) and is the one where `def deco[**P, R](func: Callable[P, R]) -> Callable[P, R]` most improves
the call-site types.

</details>

<details><summary><b>WP-02</b> — `suppress(BaseException)` swallows `KeyboardInterrupt` <i>(maintenance, low)</i></summary>

**Sites:** `services/maintenance/src/maintenance/core/lance_trace.py:149`

**Why it matters.** `references/resource-management.md` § Gotchas: "`contextlib.suppress(BaseException)`
swallows `KeyboardInterrupt` — always pass concrete `Exception` subclasses." The site wraps
`self._dropped.add(1)`, an OTel counter increment, and its docstring defends the choice of `suppress` over
`try`/`except`/`pass` (ruff SIM105) — correctly — but never addresses the `BaseException`-vs-`Exception`
half. A Ctrl-C landing exactly there is eaten. It is the only `suppress(BaseException)` in the estate;
the other 42 suppress sites name concrete types (33 of them `Exception`, which is a separate question the
error-contract epic E4 already owns).

**Fix.** `with contextlib.suppress(Exception):` — one word, and the docstring's SIM105 reasoning still holds.

</details>

---

## Appendix G — the Ray design-patterns sweep (2026-08-28)

rask's Ray usage, audited against **ray-project's own** `doc/source/ray-core/patterns/` — all 22
docs fetched from the upstream repo, 21 patterns checked (the index is not a pattern), four
adversarial agents by theme. Scope: `packages/ratch`, `services/medallion`, `packages/ray-kit`,
`scripts/ray_*_job.py`, the nine sealed runners, and the chart's Serve/RayService config.

**Headline: mostly compliant, with eight violations — and the two that mattered most were not about
Ray's scheduler at all.** The estate reaches Ray through the Jobs API rather than by driving tasks
in-process, so the whole `ray.get` family (five patterns) is structurally NOT APPLICABLE: there are
**zero `ray.get` call sites in the repo**. The `ratch` driver was singled out as a model of the
"fetch and process one batch at a time" rule, and `runners/htr`'s Serve fan-out as a correct use of
nested parallelism.

Every violation is CLOSED — each RED-first, none deferred.

| ID | Pattern | Sev | Where | What | Closed by |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `RAY-01` | `nested-tasks` | **HIGH** | `runners/htr/src/runner/transcribe_service.py` | Round-robin sharded crops, results flattened in SHARD order, zipped against entries in SORTED order — every batch of ≥4 line crops attached transcriptions to the WRONG lines, silently (`strict=True` guards length, not order) | `d680099d` |
| `RAY-02` | `concurrent-operations-async-actor` | **HIGH** | `runners/topics/deployment.py` | `async def __call__` ran the entire Toponymy build inline — minutes to hours with the replica's event loop frozen, so Serve's health probe could not be answered and the controller restarts the replica mid-build | `9be32b4b` |
| `RAY-03` | `concurrent-operations-async-actor` | med | `runners/htr/src/runner/transcribe_service.py` | `async def transcribe` had zero awaits around multi-second GPU work, making `max_ongoing_requests=2`'s documented pipelining impossible | `9be32b4b` |
| `RAY-04` | `concurrent-operations-async-actor` | low | `runners/voiceprint/voiceprint_service.py` | Speaker-encoder forward pass inline on the loop; same shape, bounded by `MAX_SAMPLES` | `9be32b4b` |
| `RAY-05` | `limit-running-tasks` | med | `runners/htr/src/runner/transcribe_service.py` | A ~4 GB TrOCR replica declared no `memory`, so the scheduler was told it costs zero bytes — the OOM cascade `pipeline.py` records was prevented only by a hand-tuned actor count | `9be32b4b` |
| `RAY-06` | `closure-capture-large-objects` | med | `packages/ratch/src/ratch/core/driver.py` | The append stage's resume filter closed over every key tuple in the OUTPUT table, cloudpickled into every task; the comment claimed "small (key tuples only)" and nothing bounded it (measured: 870 KB per task at 200k keys) | `817f0671` |
| `RAY-07` | `pass-large-arg-by-value` | low | `packages/ratch/src/ratch/core/driver.py` | `done_ids=frozenset(value_by_row_id)` copied the whole checkpoint once per actor in the pool, on the resume path | `817f0671` |
| `RAY-08` | `generators` | med | `scripts/ray_stage_job.py` | The production cascade's MEDIA branch materialised the ENTIRE dataset in the driver — one `to_table()` over every blob payload plus three more full copies — so peak RSS scaled with the dataset and the lane had an OOM ceiling nothing announced | `608bdac8` |

**Checked and NOT APPLICABLE, so the next reader need not re-audit them:** `ray-get-loop`,
`unnecessary-ray-get`, `nested-ray-get`, `ray-get-submission-order`, `ray-get-too-many-objects` (no
`ray.get` anywhere — services use the Jobs REST API and Ray Data's streaming executor makes the
intermediate-get shape structurally impossible), `global-variables`,
`out-of-band-object-ref-serialization`, `fork-new-processes`, `redefine-task-actor-loop`,
`too-fine-grained-tasks`, `limit-pending-tasks`, `tree-of-actors`, `pipelining`, `return-ray-put`,
`actor-sync`.

## Appendix F — what the re-verification changed about how to read this file

Three claims in the original text were falsified by the re-verification and have been **edited in place**
above rather than contradicted here, so no reader can take the stale version:

1. The `testpaths` half of **E9 is closed.** All 20 `services/*/tests` and `packages/*/tests` directories
   are now enrolled, and `services/search`, `services/viewer` and `packages/ratch` — recorded as shipping
   **zero tests** — now ship 167, 180 and 398 test LOC. The coverage half of E9 remains open.
2. The **"one `print()`"** count is now three.
3. The **"zero legacy typing"** claim covered only half its rule — see `WP-01`.

One thing the re-verification could NOT settle, and which a reader should know: the medallion cascade was
being actively rewritten while this audit was re-verified, and that work has since landed. Several
`medallion` findings here — `MED-004`, `MED-005` and the promotion-gate findings in particular — sit on
code it touched. Re-check those against the current tree rather than against this file.
